5. Brake Warning Relay BWR

The pressure gauge and safety valve. The gauge normally reads approximately 32 pounds when the compressor is loaded. intercooler safety valve is set for 50 pounds. Any marked deviation of intercooler pressure from 32 pounds should be reported to maintenance for attention. 

Draining of Main Reservoirs: 

The air system should be drained periodically to prevent moisture from being carried into the air brake and electric control air systems. The frequency of draining will depend on local conditions and can be determined by practice. 

It is recommended that draining be done at the time of each crew change.

LOCOMOTIVE

PART II-SECTION 6

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

Locomotives 50 and 50A were manufactured by the American Locomotive Company. The lead and booster unit each contain two 1000-horsepower, 6-cylinder engines. The cylinders are in line and operate on a four-stroke cycle arranged for direct fuel injection. The Alco turbo-charger (Buchi system) is enployed to increase the volume and pressure of the air entering the combustion chamber, depending upon the speed and load condition of the engine. Driven by the exhaust gases of engine the super charging action is entirely automatic and makes it possible for a large quantity of air and fuel oil to be conbined in the power stroke, thereby producing maximum power. 

Each engine drives a direct current generator mounted on the underframe in line with the engine, which in turn supplies the current to drive the two traction motors on the truck under each respective engine. Each engine has its own set of radiators for cooling the water and lubrication oil. The fans for the radiators are belt driven from pulleys on an extension of the main shaft. The "Pump and Nozzle Type Injection" system is employed as fuel injection medium on these power plants. (See Part 1, Section 1.) The low and high voltage equipment for control and propulsion of the locomotive are located in a compartment in the right rear of the engine room. Fuel oil and steam generator water supply tanks are located under the frame and between the motor trucks. On unit 50A four additional steam generator water tanks are located at the front of the unit. A high pressure automatic steam generator for use on the engine and train complete the components of this locomotive. 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION Control Station: 

The control station contains the locomotive throttle, movement of which increases and decreases the speed of power plants simultaneously and the reverser which changes the direction of movement of locomotive. 

A box on back side of control stand contains switches for: (See Plate 2600-3) 1. Control 2. Generator field 3. Fuel pump 

4. Attendant's call

Arranged in a line on the under side of the instrument panel are switches for: (See Plate 2600-3) 1. Headlight - bright 2. Headlight dim 30 amp. fuse 30 amp. fuse 3. Gauge and number lights 4. Defroster 5. Classification lights 10 amp. fuse 10 amp. fuse 10 amp. fuse

A red signal which lights when throttle is in Run 1 is located directly below instrument panel. 

Across the center of the panel to the front of the cab are found three duplex air gauges, left one indicating timing valve and brake application pressure, center one main reservoir and equalizing reservoir pressure, right gauge brake pipe and brake cylinder pressure. The speedometer and wheel slip light are also located here. The wheel slip light operates to indicate wheel slippage and momentary lapse of power during transition. Above the air gauges on the panel are mounted two ammeters which indicate the electrical power output of the locomotive. To the left of the ammeters is the automatic train control indicator box. On the right side of the cab, under the window, are the ATC acknowledging valve and the pneumatic control switch. 

On the left side of the cab is a panel which contains the steam generator remote controls and signals in connection with locomotive alarm circuit. 

Located at instrument panel on the right side of the cab are two load indicators having a scale which is divided into three sections, by colors, namely, white, yellow and red.

When starting the train, the indicator pointer will travel from left to right entering the red area, but as the train speed increases, the pointer will travel from right to left to the white area. As indicated on the scale, the pointer should not remain in the yellow area to exceed One 

And One-Half (1 1/2) Hours. The white area is for continuous operation. 

In the operation of this locomotive the load indicator should be closely observed in order to protect against damage to the traction motors, main generators and other electrical apparatus. When indicator pointer remains in the white area the locomotive may be operated continuously with the throttle in any position. When indicator pointer enters yellow area the locomotive may be operated with the throttle in any position for 1 1/2 hours. When indicator pointer enters red area it should be permitted to remain in that area for not to exceed FOUR MINUTES REGARDLESS OF THROTTLE POSI- 

TION.

"isolation switch" in that through this button control may be centralized at the engine itself or "put on the line" and controlled from the station in the cab. 

Above the control box are gauges to indicate fuel pressure and lubricating oil pressure. Above the gauges is a panel of lights connected with the engine alarm circuit, namely: low oil, hot engine, boiler failure and ground relay trip. In addition, a suitable bell is provided and is so wired that in case any of the signals light, it will sound.

Starting Engines: 

1. At Control Station: 

a. Place throttle in idle and reverser in neutral b. Push in fuel pump and control button. c. Set pneumatic control button. 

2. At Distribution Panel: 

a. Close main battery switch. b. See that all fuses are in place. 

3. At Engine: 

a. See that isolation switches (engine control button) in engine room are in "Off" position. b. Push in fuel pump button and note that fuel pressure rises (should be about 30 lbs.) c. Press in engine start button and hold in until lube oil pressure builds up to 30 lbs. Hold button not more than 10 seconds at a time-if it does not start after two or three trials, a check should be made for trouble. d. After lubricating oil pressure builds up, place isolation switch (engine control button) in "On" position.

Water temperature should be above 125° Before Loco motive Is Worked. 

4. To Move Locomotive: 

a. After engine operation has been checked, air pressure has built up and brakes have been tested, insert reverser handle in throttle stand. b. Check setting of pneumatic control switch button. c. Isolation switches at engines must be in "Run" position. d. Push in generator field button at control stand. e. Place reverser in desired position. f. Place foot on safety device pedal--release brakes. (Be sure hand brakes are off.) g. Operate throttle as required.

Start Train: 

In starting train, open throttle to Run 1 position. When slack is felt to be taken up, continue to open throttle one

run at a time until throttle is wide open in Run 8 position. (It is not necessary to hesitate in Run 7 position for wheel slip light except when operating in multiple with locomotive 1. Each flash of wheel slip light indicates transition of individual power plants from series to parallel. 

Operation of Transition: 

Transition from parallel to shunt and back to parallel and series to parallel is automatic in operation taking place with throttle wide open. Transition From Parallel Back To Series Is Not Automatic And Therefore It Is Necessary To Close Throttle To Idle Position When Speed Drops to 24 M.P.H. To Return To Series. Leave Throttle In Idle Position for 3 To 4 Seconds To Be Sure Change Has Been Made Back to Series. 

On heavy grades when transition has taken place with locomotive operating in parallel and full power from locomotive is desired, it will be necessary to return to series in order to "Avoid Damage To Electrical Equipment". 

When it is desired to negotiate heavy grades with contactors in series position, it will be necessary to reduce throttle when train accelerates to prevent speed from exceeding 23 M.P.H. and transition from series to parallel taking place.

Air Braking with Power: 

a. When necessary to brake the train with power still supplied by the locomotive, the throttle should be reduced to at least the 5th notch. The locomotive brakes must be be released and held in the released position when the locomotive is supplying power. As b. When preparing to stop with power applied to the locomotive and brakes applied to the train, it is necessary to reduce the throttle as the train speed decreases. the train speed slows down, the pulling power of the locomotive increases rapidly and might become great enough to part the train if the throttle is not reduced. The throttle should be in idle at least 100 ft. before the locomotive comes to a stop. 

Maximum Rating: 

In order to avoid excessive traction motor heating on passenger locomotive 50 equipped with 58/25 gear ratio, it is important that the weight of the train be kept down so that the speed is not reduced below the following value when running with wide open throttle. 

34 Miles Per Hour Continuously. 

29 Miles Per Hour For One Hour.

In general, it is advisable to climb a hill with wide open throttle in order that the time on the hill be kept as short as possible.

Steam Locomotive Used As Helper: 

When steam helper service is provided for Diesel-powered trains, the procedure necessary for the protection of the traction motors is Entirely Different From That Used Without Helpers.

As the train speed is reduced by the grade, the load indicator should be carefully observed and Diesel engine throttle must be reduced to comply with the limitations set up on the load indicator scale. In event of damage to load indicator, then throttle should be handled as indicated by the table in order to insure that more and more of the work will be done by the steam helper engine.

Correct Throttle Position

Diesel Locomotive Used As Helper: 

When operating multiple units having different shifting speeds from Parallel Back To Series (Such as 1800 H.P.Loco motive 1, With 2000 H. P. Alco Units 50 And 50A), shift 

2000 H.P. unit back to series to 24 M.P.H. and if speed continues to reduce, when 19 M.P.H. is reached shift 1800 H.P. unit back to series a. When operating 2000 H.P. Alco in multiple with 2000 H.P. 

EMC units, the transition speed series to parallel and parallel to series will be the same. 

Towing Locomotive: 

When locomotive is to be towed dead in train for any considerable distance, the reverser drums should be centered by hand into neutral position.

Close double heading cock and open dead engine feature cock. Lock brake valve in running position and set Distributing Valve Pop For Twenty-Five (25) lbs. Do not close cut out valve under independent brake valve as brakes will not release on diesel. 

Open main battery switch.

Stopping One Engine With Locomotive Running: 

1. Reduce engine speed to idle with the throttle.

Placing Engine on Line: 

Start engine in manner previously described and leave isolation switch button off or pulled out. After lube oil pressure has built up to 35 pounds, the engine may be put "on the line" With Throttle In Idle Position Only. 

Precautions During Locomotive Operation: 

1. Maintain engine cooling water temperature 140° to 160°, preferable 1500 whenever possible, and Do Not Exceed 1870. 

Engines equipped with automatic shutter controls are adjusted to open the shutters at 1500, and if for any reason the automatic shutter controls become inoperative, the shutters should be operated manually. 

2. Lubricating oil pressure should be approximately 35-50 lbs. in Run 8. If Pressure Drops Below 22 Lbs. (Operating

In Run 8 With Hot Oil) Stop Engine And Do Not Operate Until Cause Of Low Oil Pressure Is Corrected. Engines equipped with automatic shutter controls are adjusted to open the shutters at 1500 F. and, if for any reason the automatic shutter controls become inoperative, the shutters should be operated manually. 

3. Engines should not be operated when water level in expansion tank is below "Minimum Water Level Mark.

4. The battery ammeter and voltmeter are located outside the "High and Low" voltage cabinet on a panel attached to the No.2 engine cooling air tunnel and should indicate that the battery is being charged. The voltmeter should read 75 Volts and the ammeter should show a charging current of from zero to several amperes. 

5. With engine running, oil level should be maintained within 1/2 in. of high mark on bayonet gauge inside of crankcase. 

1. At Control Station: 

a. 

Close throttle to idle. b. Place reverser handle in neutral and remove handle. c. Open generator field switch. 

2. At Engine: 

a. Pull out engine control button. b. Pull out fuel pump button.

3. At Control Station: 

a. Open control switch. b. Open fuel pump switch. 

4. At Electrical Cabinet: 

a. Open main battery switch. 

5. Set up hand brake and block wheels.

TROUBLE SHOOTING If Engine Does Not Rotate When Attempt Is Made To Start: 

1. Repeat customary sequence of operations in starting engine, making sure that all controls and switches are in correct position. 

2. See that main battery switch is closed. 

3. Check all fuses affecting this circuit: a. 225 amp.-battery fuse. b. 30 amp. positive, 60 amp. negative, engine control fuses to respective engines. 

c. Locomotive control fuses. Propulsion fuse. 

4. Batteries may be too weak to turn engine over. (Note:

If lights grow dim or go out when starter is pressed batteries are weak.)

If Engine Rotates But Does Not Fire When Starter Button Is 

Pressed: 

1. Check fuel pressure. If no fuel pressure builds up: a. Check fuel pump fuse in fuse box outside "High and 

Low" voltage cabinet and fuse in engine instrument panel. b. Check to see that the fuel pump unit is running, that the shaft is not sheared, nor the coupling broken. c. See if emergency fuel valve has been tripped (located at underside of locomotive on right side just forward of the fuel tank.) d. Check fuel supply. e. Check to see that overspeed is not tripped cutting out cylinders. f. Move governor linkage manually to check for binding. g. Turn handles on fuel filters.

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle Is Opened: 

1. Check pneumatic control switch and propulsion fuse. 

2. Check to see that all brakes are released--including hand brakes. 

3. See that generator field button is in.

4. See that control air pressure is up to 80 lbs. 

5. See that ground relays are properly set. 

6. Check fuses: a. 30 amp. locomotive control fuse and 20 amp. propulsion fuse on electrical fuse panel near steam generator. 

7. See that reversers have thrown properly and contactors go in as they should. 

8. See that starting contactors are not stuck in closed position. 

Loss of Load on Engine: 

Loss of load on an engine is best determined by checking turbo-charger pressure on gauge on wall of left side of car opposite main generator. The turbo-charger pressure normally runs 4 to 5 pounds and if engine is unloaded it will not indicate more than 1 pound.

To detect the loss of load on an engine also look at the governor power piston. At idle the power piston hangs to the bottom of its travel and as engine speed is increased it will raise to load engine in proportion to each increase of speed. 

When top speed is reached the power piston will go up tight against the clevis of the servo-motor body due to the temporary overloaded condition, then as the governor and engine have balanced it will settle down and run about 1/8 in. free under full load. 

If the power piston hangs more than 1/2 in. free the unloaded engine will run cooler and it will be noted that the engine sounds like it is running "free" or without laboring. 

Engine unloading may indicate: 

1. The motors across that generator are in series while all other power plants are in parallel. 

2. The generator field or exciter field contactors are out or not making good contact. 

3. Ground relay tripped. 

4. Wheel slip relay not made. 

5. Starting contactors stuck shut. 

6. Check 30 amp. positive and 60 amp. negative engine control fuses. 

7. Check locomotive control fuses. 

8. Check propulsion fuse.

If Battery Charging Ammeter Shows Zero: 

1. Check fuses in circuit affected: a. 200 amp. auxiliary generator fuses (on left wall of cabinet). b. 225 amp. battery fuse.

Beside battery switch in bottom of electrical cabinet. 

2. Auxiliary generator belts may be loose or broken.

If Locomotive Stops in Operation: 

If locomotive suddenly becomes inoperative check:

1. 30 amp. positive and 60 amp. negative engine and locomotive control fuses on panel, and 20 amp. propulsion fuse, also pneumatic control switch may have opened. 

2. Control air pressure may have dropped. 

3. Fuel flow may have stopped. 

4. Ground relays may have tripped.

If Engine Fails to Speed Up When Throttle Is Opened: 

1. Isolation switch may be out. 

2. Control air pressure may be low. 

3. Check setting of pneumatic control switch. 

4. Control push button may be out.. 

5. Ground relay may be tripped. 

6. Oil pressure switch may not be closed. 

7. 30 amp. engine and locomotive control fuses may be blown out. 

8. Fuel supply may have stopped.

Engine Alarm and Signal System: 

These are located on the back wall of the cab and over the two gauge panels adjacent to each Diesel engine. Also in cab on some locomotives. 

1. Low oil pressure 

(yellow). (red). (green). 

2. High water temperature 

3. Steam generator failure 

The two panels adjacent to the Diesel engines also have a white signal for ground relay indicator.

In addition, a bell is located near each of the Diesel engines and is wired to ring in conjunction with the lights. 

This bell may also be operated from a push button on cab control stand to signal engine room. 

SECTION 2 ENGINE COOLING SYSTEM Description: 

The cooling system of each engine on locomotive 50 and 50A are separate and the capacity is 250 gallons for each engine. The engine and radiators are full at all times and the water is circulated through the system by a centrifugal pump driven off of fuel pump drive and located on the exhaust side of engine at generator end.

A dial type thermometer is located in the outlet header of cooling system and a thermal relay operating in conjunction with alarm system is provided to determine cooling water temperature.

The cooling water temperature is controlled by shutters on the outside of the cooling radiators which are automatically operated by pneumatic control valves through the operation of a thermal relay in the radiator water line. 

Temperatures should be carried between 1400 And 1600 

Not To Exceed 1870. 

To Fill Cooling System: 

1. Although the cooling systems are separate from each other, they are filled from the same filler pipe located on the middle right side of locomotive, directly behind the fuel tank under the edge of the car. The hose should be engaged to the filler pipe and water turned on, then fill each system individually by opening a valve located on the respective side of pipe "T" inside the locomotive in back of the engine room door on right side of unit. Fill each system until water appears at the expansion tank vent pipe on roof. After systems are full Close The Valve leading to the "T" and the intake pipe. Check and see that other drain valves in system are closed. 

2. Never add cold water to a hot engine unless hot water is not available and then add very slowly. 

3. Water may be added thru vent pipe in the roof above the expansion tank, but care must be exercised to avoid getting water into turbo-charger or air duct intakes. 

4. The expansion tank of each system is located in the roof beside the engine on the left side of the unit. Each expansion tank has a water glass on the side of tank toward the engine to indicate level of water in the tank. This glass has a minimum water level mark which must be observed. 

Operate An Engine If Level Of Water In The Expansion Tank Is Below Minimum Water Level Mark (3/4 Glass). 

To Drain Cooling System; 

Never 

1. Open the drain valve in filler line of the engine to be drained located between the engine and the "T" in back of the engine room door on right side of car. 

2. Open engine block drain valve located on the floor in the center of the front part of the engine on the same side with the engine fuel pump. 

3. Remove the plug in the bottom of the water pump housing. 

4. The entire water system of an engine should be drained in the event it must be shut down in freezing weather.

Radiator Cooling Air: 

There is a set of cooling radiators and a radiator fan for each Diesel engine. Air is drawn through the radiators, which are located on the sides of the locomotive, and blown out through the roof by a fan mounted in a horizontal position in the roof above the air compressor. 

SECTION 3 LOCOMOTIVE LUBE OIL SYSTEM Description: 

The complete system includes the strainers and filters for cleaning the oil and protecting the equipment, the oil radiators for relieving heat of the engine from the oil and the pumps for moving the oil. The oil capacity of the system is 120 gallons per engine and level should be maintained within 1/2 in. of "high" level on bayonet gauge in side of crankcase. (Under fuel pump assembly.)

The system is drained by a pipe leading out of the engine sump. 

The oil supply is replenished through a suitable filler cavity located at front end of engine on the exhaust side. 

It is necessary to unscrew the cover from the hole and oil should be poured with engine shut down. 

Low Oil Pressure and Alarm Switch: 

Lube oil pressure runs approximately 35 to 50 lbs. (In 

Run 8 with hot oil.)

Two protective switches are provided against oil pressure failure. When the pressure falls to 22 lbs. the first switch operates to reduce the engine speed to idle and open the generator field circuit to failing power plant if the throttle is in 5th to 8th notches. If the pressure falls to 

8 lbs. the second switch operates to shut down the diesel engine. If either switch functions the signal lights and alarm bells will operate (except 1 to 4 throttle positions on 22 pound switch).

SECTION 4 LOCOMOTIVE FUEL SYSTEM Description: 

A 1200-gallons fuel storage tank is mounted beneath the locomotive frame between the trucks. The fuel supply for both power plants and the steam generator is drawn from this tank.

Fuel is drawn from the main tank through the duplex strainer to the intake side of the auxiliary pump; then out of the discharge side of the pump through the Cuneo filter assembly into the fuel oil intake manifold. This fuel manifold supplies the fuel injector pumps mounted together in a battery on exhaust side of the engine. Here each pump forces fuel under high pressure to the nozzle in each cylinder head. 

An overflow fuel line returns unused fuel to the tank.

This line is equipped with a 35-lb. relief valve to hold back pressure on fuel in manifold. The fuel pressure gauge on instrument panel indicates the amount of this back pressure and usually runs from 30 to 40 lbs.

Emergency Fuel Cut Off Valve (See General Section All Loco motives)

The reverser is an air operated reversing switch. Its purpose is to change the direction of flow of current through the traction motor fields, thus reversing the rotation of the traction motors. The current does not reverse in the main generator. If the control air pressure is low (should be 

80 lbs.) or either of the magnet valves on top of the reverser is inoperative the reverser will not operate properly. 

Sequence Relays (AVR BVR CVR DVR EVR)

These relays are located in the nose of the lead unit and above water tank in booster units. These relays are operated from the throttle in the same manner as the engine magnet valves. They control excitation to main generator to give a "soft start" and gradual increase in speed to provide smooth train handling.

HIGH AND LOW VOLTAGE CABINET 

The high and low voltage equipment for controlling and operating each unit is enclosed in a cabinet in the rear of the unit opposite the steam generator. (The booster unit cabinet has a partition in the center of the cabinet to separate the apparatus for each respective power plant-the lead unit does not have this partition.) The following apparatus is contained in this cabinet: 

Battery Charging Contactor--BC 

The battery charging contactor is of the magnetic type and is used to connect the storage battery to the auxiliary generator for charging purposes.

Transition Relays--V1 V21 These control relays operate automatically as generator amperage and voltage output fluctuates. These relays operate the traction motor contactors to obtain the desired tractive effort and speed within the voltage operating limit of the generator. 

The transition from series to parallel is brought about by the transfer and field shunting relay operation. 

When locomotive speed reaches the transition point for field shunting operation, the transfer and field shunting relay "V" again picks up, and this time connects resistors which shunt some of the current out of the motor fields. When, due to grade conditions, deceleration occurs, the increased current and falling voltage will cause relays to operate to return traction motor connections to "parallel" from "reduced field."

No provision is made for making transition from parallel to series and this must be accomplished by returning throttle to idle.

Control Relay-OPR 

Shuts engine down.

Exciter Field Contactor--EF1 EF21 

Connects exciter field to battery to energize exciter generator field.

Fuel Pump Contactor--FPC 

This contactor is located in the high voltage cabinet and is controlled from main fuel pump push button in cab. 

When this contactor is in, the fuel (auxiliary) pumps may be controlled from the fuel pump button at engine start panels. Generator Field Contactor--GF1 GF21 This contactor connects the main generator shunt field to the exciter. It opens during transition from series to parallel to reduce generator output and prevent arcing of contactors. Engine Starting Contactors--GS1 GS21 The starting contactors connect battery to main generator for cranking the diesel engine. They are of the magnetic type. There are two for each power plant and are interlocked so as to be used only by pushing start button with engine isolating button in "start" position. Reverse Current Relay--RCR This relay prevents battery current from motoring auxiliary generator by opening battery charging contactor when auxiliary generator voltage drops below battery voltage. 

Wheel Slip Relay--WS 

This relay is provided to measure the difference in voltage between the respective motors when operating in

series connection. In the event either wheel slips, its motor voltage increases and the difference in voltage causes the relay to be energized operating warning lights and unloading main generator. It prevents transition to parallel during slipping but is inoperative in parallel connection. 

The relay operates momentarily whenever a power plant makes transition so a brief flash will denote transfer taking place. 

When wheels slip, it is necessary to throttle down until wheels stop slipping and gradually reapply load. If necessary to use sand, it should be applied (sparingly) only after wheels stop slipping.

Voltage Regulator--Xl 

This relay is a dynamic type, voltage regulating device, to maintain constant auxiliary generator voltage to batteries throughout the operating speed range of the engine, regardless of gravity of battery. A defective voltage regulator will be evidenced by excessive fluctuations of ammeter above fuse panel. 

Main Battery Switch 

Isolates all circuits from battery except external charging circuit. 

Traction Motor Pneumatic Contactors-Pl S P2 

These contactors change the traction motor connections from series to parallel with main generator. The series and parallel contactors are interlocked so that the parallel contactors can not operate while series contactors are closed and vice versa.

Traction Motor Field Shunting Contactors--M1 M2 M3 M4 

They are used for connecting the motor field shunting resistors across traction motor field. They are under control of voltage control and field shunting relays. 

Ground Protective Relay--GPR 

If a ground occurs in high voltage circuit, this relay will open generator field contactor, unload the engine, operate ground relay light and ring bell. Should this occur, isolate the engine and reset the relay with reset lever. 

Place throttle in idle and put engine on the line then come out on throttle-if relay repeatedly opens when engine speed is increased-leave power plant isolated. 

Time Delay Relay-TDR 

This relay is used in traction motor field shunting circuits. It inserts a one second time delay in operation when going back from field shunting to full field motor connections. Its purpose is to give any possible surges time to die out before the generator field shunting relay can pick up again.

Battery Charging Switch: 

Isolates all battery charging equipment (auxiliary generator, reverse current relay, battery charging contactor and voltage regulator from the battery when switch is open). 

The following fuses are located within the electrical cabinet: 

2 steam generator fuses-100 amp. 

2 external battery charging receptacle fuses-60 amp. 

No. 1 and No. 2 auxiliary generator fuse--200 amp. 

225 ampere main battery fuse (located beside main battery switch). Signal Relay-SR This relay operates with the signal lights (steam generator, hot engine, low oil and GPR) to ring the alarm bell.

SECTION 6 AIR SYSTEM 

Air Compressor: 

The two cylinder, compound type air compressor is driven directly from main engine crankshaft of each engine supplying the compressed air for air brake and control air systems. The compressor is installed on the front end of the diesel engine and its operation is controlled by a mechanical governor located on the left side of unit near No. 2 compressor. The governor is set to maintain main reservoir pressure between 130 to 140 pounds.

Draining Air Reservoirs: 

The air reservoirs are on each side with drain valves at the bottom of each tank. In order to prevent moisture being carried into the air brake and control air system, these reservoirs should be drained at each crew change.

SECTION 7 MISCELLANEOUS EQUIPMENT Couplers: 

Coupler in front of Locomotive 50 is of swivel type and swings into position after cover is removed. A locking block on right side of coupler must be moved into position against drawbar to protect side movement of coupler drawbar. 

To Uncouple Units: 

1. Take down both jumper cables on the inside of car between the units. (It is only necessary to take down one end of cable but pull cable back into unit so it will not be damaged when units are parted.) 

2. Set air on unit to remain stationary. 

3. Close stop valves to steam line and disconnect the steam line. 

4. Close angle cocks and disconnect all of the air lines between the units. 

5. Remove key from uncoupling pin of coupler and open coupler with provided lever. 

6. Release air on unit to be moved and operate as desired. 

NOTE: Push in control and fuel pump button on booster (50A) unit hostler's control at time cable is removed and push in generator field switch if booster is to be operated alone.

Santa Fe 

DIESEL ELECTRIC PASSENGER LOCOMOTIVES 

CLASS 51 (51-58; 63-74)

CLASS 59 (59-62; 75-78) 

SANTA FE PART II-SECTION 7

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

The 51 and 59 class passenger locomotives were built by The American Locomotive Company and with General Electric Company supplying the electrical equipment. The locomotive is powered by a Model 244-B, V type, 16 cylinder, engine.

The engine speeds are controlled by an electro-hydraulic 

General Electric Governor, Model 17 MG. The generator is mounted directly to the engine crankshaft through a flexible coupling. The auxiliary generators and amplidyne exciter are mounted to the main generator and gear driven from the free end of the generator. One auxiliary generator furnishes current for the traction motor blowers and the other furnishes current for the low voltage system. 

The locomotives were originally built as 3-unit, 6000 hp. locomotives with two cab units and one booster unit per locomotive, and numbered from 51 through 62. Later more units were purchased with two units per locomotive. The original 3-unit locomotives were reduced to 2-unit locomotives with the extra cab unit being renumbered. This accounts for the break in the locomotive class numbers. 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION 

These locomotives must not be operated in excess of 100 m.p.h. The locomotive is equipped with an overspeed control switch which will automatically set the brakes if this speed is exceeded. 

The control station contains the necessary equipment and controls for operation of the locomotive. (See Plate 2700-1).

The transition meter (load indicator) shown at control station is an ammeter in the No.1 traction motor circuit and indicates the current flowing through the traction motors. If for any reason the No.1 traction motor is cut out, the transition meter will be inoperative. 

The "PC" switch operates when an automatic train control, automatic train stop, safety device pedal, locomotive overspeed, or emergency brake application is made. The operation of the "PC" switch will reduce the Diesel engine speed to idle. ΤΟ regain control of the locomotive, return the throttle to idle until the brake application has been recovered. If the brakes are applied in "Service", the brake valve should be placed in the lap position and left there until "PC" switch resets and air is recovered. After a penalty application, place the brake valve in Lap, close throttle to idle, depress safety device pedal, allow application pipe pressure to build up to main reservoir pressure and release brakes.

The controls on the left side of cab are the steam generator controls as shown in Plate 2700-2.

PLATE 2700-1 CONTROL STATION

The contactor compartment panels are located in the cab of lead units and on the high voltage cabinet of booster units. Plate 2700-3. 

The main battery switch and battery trainline switch are located at the left and below the engine instrument panel. (See Plate 2700-4). 

To Start Engine: 

1. Close main battery switch. 2.

Proceed to operating cab and close all circuit breakers on Contactor Compartment Panel. 

3. Place selector handle in OFF, throttle in OFF and reverser in OFF position. 

4. Place safety device pedal cut-out cock in cut-out position. (If no air pressure on system). 

5. Close Control and Fuel Pump Circuit breakers on control stand. 

6. Close panel light and fuel pump switches located on engine control panel. (Allow fuel pressure to build up to 30 psi.) 

7. Turn Engine Control knob to Idle position. (Low Oil 

Pressure light will light and alarm bell will ring until engine is started). 

8. Wait 10 seconds for power plant governor to reset. 

9. Turn START switch on engine control panel to crank engine. Hold START switch closed after engine fires until oil pressure builds up to approximately 20 psi. and Low Oil Pressure light goes out and alarm bell stops ringing. (While engine is being cranked observe the engine fuel pump racks. They should come out when engine is being cranked.) 

10. Check lube oil pressure gage on engine control panel.

Should have a minimum of 20 psi. 

11. Open crankcase exhauster circuit breaker located on contactor panel. 

12. Check engine lube oil level should be within 1/2 in. of high mark on bayonet gage. 

13. Close crankcase exhauster circuit breaker.

To Move Locomotive: 

1. Open safety device pedal cut-out cock. 

2. Turn Engine Control knob to Run position, pausing 3 

seconds in each position. 

3. Close Generator Field switch on control stand. 

4. Move reverse handle to position for direction desired. 

5. Move selector handle to No. 4 position for automatic transition. (Automatic transition can be forestalled by placing selector handle in highest transition desired). 

6. Place foot on safety device pedal. 

7. Release brakes. 

8. Open throttle as required.

Operation of Transition Lever: 

This locomotive is equipped with automatic transition and will make transition automatically both forward and backward as long as the transition lever is kept in the No. 4 position. Automatic transition is made on the basis of locomotive speed so is NOT dependent on main generator voltage or amperage in making transition as are the E.M.D. locomotives. 

To hold the locomotive in any transition position, move the lever to that position. The locomotive will make transittion automatically to that position but not beyond. For instance, if the transition lever is placed in the No. 2 position, the locomotive will make transition from 1 to 2 and 2 to 1 automatically. 

Transition will normally take place automatically:

51 Class 

1 to 2 26 m.p.h. 2 to 3 33 m.p.h. 3 to 4 63 m.p.h. 59 Class 26 m. p.h. 33 m.p.h. 63 m.p.h. 

When operating on grades that level off slightly causing the locomotive to make transition forward one step and then return almost immediately, place the transition lever in the position at which the locomotive operates a major portion of the time. This will forestall undesired forward transition.

The short time ratings shown above for the 51 and 59 classes are not to be exceeded under any circumstances. When the indicator pointer enters the red area during the operation of a train, the locomotive may be operated only for the minimum allowable time (or distance) as indicated by the pointer, the short time ratings are not to be accumulative. 

For instance, the pointer of a 59 class locomotive levels off at 1250 amps. This means that the locomotive may be operated for 6 minutes or 1.0 miles at that rating. 

After any one of the above ratings has been used, the load on the locomotive must be reduced a sufficient amount so that the pointer moves out of the overload area for at least 15 minutes before again entering the overload area. 

Whenever any of the power wheels slip on the locomotive, the indicator on the instrument panel will light. When wheel slippage occurs, sand should be used and throttle should not be reduced. The wheel slip device automatically reduces the power on the unit on which the wheel slippage occurs and eliminates the need for reducing the throttle.

The wheel slip light will not burn continuously because wheels do not slip continuously. The connections from the wheel slip relay to the power plant are such that the power on the unit is automatically reduced when the wheels slip. 

Therefore, wheels will slip, then stop, slip again, and stop about once a second.

A steady glow of the wheel slip light indicates electrical trouble and should be investigated immediately. 

Air Braking With Power: 

When it is necessary with full power applied to the locomotive, the throttle should be reduced to at least the sixth notch. It is imperative that locomotive brakes be released and held in the release position when the locomotive is supplying power.

Train line battery switch 

Main battery switch

PLATE 2700-4 BATTERY SWITCHES

When preparing to stop with power applied to the locomotive and brakes applied to the train, reduce the throttle setting as the train speed drops. The throttle should be in idle before the locomotive stops. This is because the pulling power (tractive effort) of the locomotive greatly increases as the speed drops.

These instructions are not to interfere with the operating instructions covering the use of dynamic or air brakes. 

Emergency Stop Button: 

The red button on the engine control stand in the cab of the locomotive is to be used to shut down all engines of the locomotive in case of emergency.

This shuts the engines down in such a manner that all engines must be isolated before any engine can again be started. This is done by turning each engine control switch to the idle position. Start engines in the usual manner. 

Dynamic Brake: 

The dynamic brake is an electrical retarding device which utilizes the traction motors as generators. This action creates a retarding force on the train. The electrical energy generated by the traction motors is dissipated as heat by the dynamic brake grids.

To apply the dynamic brake, first close the throttle lever to idle, after waiting 10 seconds, move the transition selector handle to the OFF position. At this position the loadbraking pointer should show a slight movement. Disengage the transition selector handle and move it slowly into the "B" position. Stop this movement when the pointer reaches 100 amps. This should produce enough braking effort to bunch the slack of the train. When slack is bunched apply the brake as needed within the limits of the locomotive. 

The 51 class locomotives have 970 amp. dynamic braking limit, while the 59 class locomotives have an 800 amp. dynamic braking limit. If these units are operated together, operate the locomotive in accordance with the instructions for the cab unit in the lead of the locomotive. The dynamic brake may be operated up to the allowable maximum amperage as stated above and as shown on the Load Indicator Meter. However, in the event a brake warning light indication, reduce the dynamic brake until the light goes out. DO NOT OPERATE in the red zone as shown on the meter or with the warning light on. 

If it is impossible to maintain desired train speed by use of the dynamic brake only, it is permissible to use the train brakes in conjunction with the dynamic brake. However, be sure to keep locomotive brakes RELEASED AT ALL TIMES when using the dynamic brake.

Below 27 m.p.h. the available braking effort of the locomotive diminishes. Therefore, when coming to a stop, it is necessary to release the dynamic brake and apply the independent brake sufficiently to prevent a run-out of the locomotive. Complete the stop in the prescribed manner. 

Differences in idling speeds of the engines of the locomotive may cause the warning light to come on before maximum allowable braking effort is reached. At the first indication of overload, reduce the dynamic braking effort. Failure to do so will result in damage to the grids or traction motors. 

DO NOT PLACE AN ENGINE on the line while in dynamic braking. 

The dynamic brake on any unit can be cut-out by moving the engine control switch to the idle position. THIS SHOULD 

NOT BE DONE, except in cases of extreme emergency, while the dynamic brake is in use.

To avoid surges on the equipment and on the train, the dynamic brakes should not be cut in or out on any unit except when the selector handle (transition lever) is in the OFF position. 

A dynamic brake interlock is provided between the locomotive air brakes and the dynamic brake. This interlock prevents the application of the locomotive brakes when an automatic brake application is made while the dynamic brake is in use. When an emergency application occurs from any cause, the interlock is nullified and full air pressure is applied to the locomotive brake cylinder. 

When using the dynamic brake and an emergency application is made, return the selector handle to the OFF position immediately. 

Operating With Steam Locomotive Helper: 

Steam locomotive tonnage ratings are often based on speeds lower than the continuous rating speed of the Diesel. It is, therefore, necessary to be careful that the Diesel locomotive does not pull more than its share of the tonnage and overheat the traction motors. The Diesel locomotive may be operated at speeds at or above 23 m. p.h. at full throttle. If the grade and/or tonnage are so great that the locomotive will not maintain this speed, it will be necessary to reduce the throttle according to the chart shown below: Throttle 8. 7. 6. 5. m. .p.h. 23 & up 18 to 23 .14.5 to 18 .11 to 14.5

Towing Locomotive: 

When the locomotive is to be moved dead in train or towed by other power in yard movements, place the throttle in the idle position and move the reverser handle to the OFF position, and remove the handle from the control stand. 

If the locomotive is moved dead in train, take the following action: 

Lead Units: 

(a) Close brake pipe cut-out cock and remove automatic brake valve handle in running position. (b) Place dead engine cock in Dead position on D-24 brake control valve. (c) Place charging change over cock in Passenger position on D-24 control valve. (d) Remove or keep independent brake valve handle in 

Release position. (e) Place Rotair valve handle in Passenger position. 

Booster Units: 

(a) Place dead engine cock in Dead position on D-24 control valve. (b) Open brake valve cut-out cock (hostler's station).

(c) Remove brake valve handle in Full Application posi tion, then move brake valve stem to Release position, using Reverse side of brake valve handle. (d) Place charging change over and control emergency cocks in Passenger position. (e) Close reducing valve cut-out cock. (f) Open end cocks on actuating and application pipes on end of unit. 

release If the locomotive or unit is to be moved dead in train during freezing weather, drain the engine cooling system and steam generators of water to prevent freezing of the equipment. 

Isolating an Engine While Under Power: 

To isolate an engine while under power, turn the E.C. (Engine Control) switch located on the engine control panel to idle position. In doing this, pause at No. 6, No. 4 and 

No. 2 throttle positions to allow time for the engine to unload and reduce speed gradually. If the engine is to be shut down, follow the instructions given under "Stopping An engine".

Precaution During Locomotive Operation: 

(a) Maintain engine cooling water temperature 150° to 

180° F., preferably 1650 F. whenever possible. DO NOT 

EXCEED 198° F. Engines are equipped with automatic shutter control which will normally control engine cooling water temperature. If they do not control temperatures correctly, the shutters may be operated manually. Engine temperature should be up to 1250 F. before engine is operated under load. (b) Lubricating oil pressure should be approximately 35 to 50 psi. operating in Run 8 with hot oil. If pressure drops below 22 psi. (operating in Run 8 with hot oil) stop engine and do not operate until the cause of the low oil pressure is located. (c) Engines should not be operated with cooling water below the minimum water level mark on the expansion tank (3/4 glass). (d) With the engine running, the oil level should be maintained with 1/2 in. of high mark on bayonet gage in crankcase. (e) If a circuit breaker should trip out, the handle will be midway between the ON and OFF position. To reset, move the switch handle to the OFF position and then to the ON position. This should reset the circuit breaker. In some cases it may be necessary to wait a few minutes before resetting the breaker.

Stopping Engines: 

1. Snap Generator Field switch located on control stand to OFF position. 

2. Turn Engine Control knob to OFF position. 

3. Turn Fuel Pump switch OFF. 

4. Snap all other switches on control stand and engine control panel to OFF. Open main battery switch. 

TROUBLE SHOOTING If Fuel Pump Does Not Run: 

1. Check all circuit breakers in circuit. (a) On control stand. (b) On control compartment panel. (c) On engine control panel. 

If Fuel Pump Runs But Engine Does Not Receive Fuel: 

1. Check supply and emergency fuel cut-off valve. 

2. Check for loose connections in fuel line, particularly on suction side of the fuel pump.

1. Check the following circuit breakers: 

(a) Check positive and negative control circuit breakers on distribution panel. (b) Check control circuit breaker on control stand. 

2. Check battery switch located directly beneath engine control panel. 

If Engine Rotates But Does Not Fire: 

1. Check to see that fuel pump is running and that fuel pressure shows at least 30 psi. 

2. Check to see that overspeed reset button, is in. If button is out, turn control switch OFF and reset over speed button. 

3. When cranking engine, observe fuel racks. If they do not come out, release Start switch and turn control to OFF. After a few seconds return engine control switch to idle and repeat starting procedure. 

4. Check to see that some fuel injector pumps are not cut out. 

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle is Opened: 

1. Check for released brakes. 

2. See that Generator Field switch at Control Stand is in. 

3. See that ground relays are set in normal position and that engine control switch is in run position. 

4. Check the following circuit breakers: (a) Motor generator set. (b) Blower generator field. (c) Blower generator. 

4. Check control air pressure.

If Engines Do Not Load Up: 

(a) Check ground relay reset by pushing in on button located on enclosed contactor panel in upper right of high voltage cabinet. If relay operates repeatedly, isolate the engine and do not use dynamic brake until trouble is corrected. (b) Check to see if starting contactors are stuck shut.

PC switch not reset.

If The Engine Fails to Stop: 

If the engine fails to stop when stop button is pressed, then turn fuel pump switch off.

If Traction Motor Blowers Stop: 

The alarm bells will ring in all units and the ground relay light will light on the unit effected. However, the red target will not show on the ground relay panel. 

Check blower generator circuit breaker. 

Check blower generator field circuit breaker. 

Check motor generator circuit breaker. 

If the generator circuit breaker is tripped and the engine is running above idle speed, DO NOT reset the generator circuit breaker while the generator field circuit breaker is in. 

If the blowers are not running and the circuit breakers are closed, take the engine OFF THE LINE. The engine may be run at idle speed to protect from freezing but not under power.

A single traction motor blower failure is sufficient to necessitate taking the engine off the line. 

SECTION 2 COOLING SYSTEM Description: 

The cooling water circulated by a pump flows to the right and left bank inlet headers from which it enters the cylinder liner jacket. The cooling water then enters the cylinder heads and out through elbows which are connected to the water outlet header. Water than flows to the radiators, cab heaters and expansion tank. It is a closed system with the expansion tank vented to the atmosphere. (See Plate 2700-7).

The water expansion tank is located at the highest point. on the system and is equipped with a sight glass and overflow pipe. The engine should be operated with the sight glass showing 3/4 full. However, the engines may be operated as long as there is any water showing in the sight glass. 

Filling Water System: 

The system may be filled under a maximum pressure of 25 psi. by connecting a hose to the filler nozzle and opening the filler drain valve. The system may also be filled by gravity through the roof filling connection.

Draining Water System: 

Open filler drain valve and inlet and outlet valves on cab heaters. This will allow all water to drain from engine, oil cooler, radiators and cab heaters. Water will not drain from volute casing of water pump, so plug in bottom of pump must be removed to drain water from pump. 

Hot Engine Alarm Switch: ETS 

The hot engine alarm signal is set for 1980 F. When water temperature is excessive the engine speed is reduced to idle, the hot engine alarm will come on and a red indicator light will show in the cab. If and when the temperature is reduced to normal operating range, the engine is automatically returned to the line and assumes its normal speed.

The manual shutter control is shown on Plate 2700-8. 

The manual fan control is located on the Engine Control and Instrument Panel.

To operate the shutters manually, close the shutter control air cut-out cock and open "T" vent valve. Move shutter levers to maintain desired temperature. 

To operate the fan speed manually, move the knurled radiator fan control switch from the idle position to the speed setting desired. 

SECTION 3 LUBE OIL SYSTEM Description: 

The engine lubricating oil system is a self contained unit in which all working parts are pressure lubricated by a gear type pump driven from the engine crankshaft. The engine is a wet sump type in that all lubricating oil is stored in the engine base. Lube oil is pumped from the engine base through the oil filter, cooler and strainer before it is pumped back through the engine. 

Lube Oil Pressure: 

Lube oil pressure should vary between 35 and 50 psi. when operating the engine in Run 8. If the pressure drops to 20 psi., the low oil pressure switch returns the engine to idle. 

If the oil pressure drops to 7 psi. the engine is shut down. (See Plate 2700-9).

Low Oil Pressure Switch: 

Each diesel engine is protected from low lubricating oil pressure by two switches. The first switch OPSI is set for 

20 psi and returns the engine to idle when the throttle is in the fifth, sixth, seventh or eighth notch while the second switch OPS 2 set for 7 psi. will shut the engine down in all throttle positions. If the lubricating oil pressure should drop below 20 psi. and remain above 7 psi. the engine may be operated at partial load by turning the engine control knob on the engine control panel to No. 4 position. 

SECTION 4 FUEL SYSTEM Description: 

The engine on each unit has its own separate fuel system. 

The fuel tank is located under the floor of each unit and can be filled from either side of the locomotive. 

A fuel oil booster pump draws fuel from the tank and distributes it through the fuel headers. See Plate 2700-10.

The fuel is drawn from the tank and is then discharged at 35 to 40 psi. by the pump into the pressure filters, from which it flows to the fuel headers. The pressure regulating valve located at the end of the left bank header controls the pressure in both headers.

Emergency Fuel Cut-Off Valve: 

(See General Section All Locomotive) Fuel Booster Pump: 

The fuel booster governor pump assembly consists of an electric motor driving a gear type fuel booster pump through a flexible coupling on one and of its shaft and a gear type governor oil pump through another flexible coupling on the other end of its shaft. The brushes for the motor are located at the fuel pump end of the motor. וי

This section deals with the description and function of the electric equipment on this class of locomotives. 

Time Delay Relay (AR): 

Co-operating with field shunting control relay causes shunting one notch at a time. Sequence Relays (AVF-AVS-BVS-CVF-CVS-DVS): 

Commutates resistance in governor speed coil and current limit control circuits. 

Battery Contactor (B): 

This contactor connects the battery to the auxiliary generator. 

Braking Contactors (B1-B2): 

Connects traction motor armatures to braking resistors.

Blower Stopped Relay (BLR) : 

Energizes alarm circuit indicating lack of power.

Braking Interlocks (BR1-BR2): 

Sets up control circuits for dynamic brakes.

Brake Overload Relay (BWR) : 

Energizes warning light on a dynamic brake overload.

Motor Cut Out Switch (C.O.): 

This switch used to cut out traction motor if it becomes necessary.

Automatic Transition Relays (CR1-CR2-CR3): 

These relays control the automatic transition shift from No.1 to 2, from No.2 to 3, from No.3 to 4.

Field Shunting Controller (CS): 

Shunts traction motor fields for dynamic brake.

Capacitor (CSC): 

Prevents arcing of IP1 contacts in CS.

Auxiliary Relay (CSD): 

"DOWN". 

Closes circuit turning multi-pointed shunter "OFF" or 

Auxiliary Relay CSU): "UP". 

Closes circuit turning multi-pointed shunter "ON" or 

Dynamic Brake Magnet Valve (DBM): 

Provides interlock between dynamic brake and independent air brake.

Deadman's Relay (DMR): 

Opens control circuits removing power.

Engine Control Switch (EC): 

This switch controls stop and start of engine and limits engine speed to whatever throttle position switch is set for.

Exciter Field Relay (EF): 

Energizes transformer to furnish excitation for amplidyne exciter. 

Engine Stop Relay (ESR): 

This relay stops engine by opening the governor clutch coil circuit. 

Engine Temperature Switch (ETS): 

This switch idles engine, lights hot engine light and rings alarm.

Fuel Pump Contactor (FPC) : 

Energizes motor-operated fuel pump.

Generator Field Contactor (GF): 

Connects main generator field with amplidyne exciter armature. 

Governor Oil Pressure Switch (GOP): 

Shuts engine down if governor oil pressure fails.

Ground Relay (GR): 

This relay removes power, idles engine, lights the warning light and rings the alarm whenever energized by a grounded condition.

Cranking Contactors (GS1-GS2): 

Connects the starting winding in the main generator to the batteries for starting diesel engine. 

Locomotive Overspeed Switch (LOS): 

Returns engine to idle and applies air brakes whenever maximum permissible speed is exceeded. 

Speed Relays (LS1-LS2-LS3): 

Control the automatic-transition relays CR1-CR2-CR3 respectively. 

Field Shunting Contactors (M1-M2-M3-M4): 

Connects resistance across traction motor fields.

Oil Pressure Relay (OPR): 

Idles engine, lights warning light, rings alarm bell on low lube oil pressure or hot engine.

Low Lube Oil Pressure Switch (OPS1): 

Idles engine if lube oil pressure drops to 20 lbs. in 

5,6,7 or 8th notch.

Low Lube Oil Pressure Switch (OPS2): 

Shuts engine down if lube oil pressure drops to 7 lbs. in any notch.

Engine Overspeed Switch (OS): 

Shuts engine down when engine RPM reaches 1100.

Overspeed Magnet Valve (OSM): 

Cuts out dynamic braking and applies air brakes on locomotive when locomotive overspeeds. 

Parallel Motor Contactors (Pl-P2-P21-P22): 

Connects the traction motors across the main generator.

Pneumatic Control Switch (PCS): 

Pneumatic switch energizes DMR.

Exciter Field Rectifier (R1): 

Supplies DC for amplydine fields F1-F2.

Gen. Field Mod. Rectifier (R2): 

Supplies DC for generator field limit control.

Gen. Field Mod. Rectifier (R3): 

Supplies DC-for generator load limit control.

Reverse Current Relay (RC): 

Control battery charging contactor. 

Reverser (Rev.): 

Controls the direction of the flow of current to the traction motors. 

Safety Relay (SAR): 

Opens governor clutch coil if speed coil circuit fails.

Shutter Magnet Valve (SMV) : 

Controls air admission to shutter system.

Signal Relay (SR): 

Energizes alarm circuits.

Connects power circuit for dynamic braking.

Series Motor Contactor (S1-S21): 

Connects No.1 and 3 motors in series S21 connects No.2 

and 4 motors in series.

Transfer Relay (TR): 

Opens excitation circuits during transition.

Transformer (T1): 

Supplies power for field circuit, field limit and current limit control. 

Traction Motor Blower Motors (TB1-TB2): 

Furnishes cooling air to traction motors.

Wheel Slip Relays (WS1-WS2-WS3): 

Energizes buzzer, lights warning light if wheels slip.

Voltage Control Relay (X): 

Regulates voltage output of auxiliary generator. 

Operation of Relays and Contactors during Transition: 

1. Shift from Series-Parallel to Series Parallel Shunt

a. LS1 relay is picked up by the output of the axle generator. b. LS1 relay picks up CR1 relay. c. CR1 relay passes current through S21 and P2 interlocks to M1 and M4 field shunting contactor operating coils. d. Ml interlock closes to pick up M2 and M3. 

2. Shift from Series Parallel Shunt to Parallel a.

LS2 relay is picked up by output of the axle generator. b. LS2 relay picks up CR2 relay. c. CR2 drops out CR1. d. CR1 opens, drops out M1 and M4. e. Ml interlock opens drops out M2 and M3. f. M2's interlock closes to pick up T.R. relay. g. TR's contact opens to drop out EF and GF. h. GF's interlock closes to pick up Pl and P21. i. Pl's interlock closes to lock in P1 and P21. j. Pl and P21's interlocks open to drop out S1 and S21. k.

S1 and S21's interlocks close to pick up P2 and P22. 

1. P2's interlock closes to lock in TR. m. P22's interlock closes to pick up EF and GF. 

3. Shift from Parallel to Parallel Shunt. a. LS3 picks up from output of axle generator. b. LS3's contact picks up CR3. c. CR3's contact closes to feed through TR's contact to pick up M1 and M4. d. Ml's interlock picks up M2 and M3. 

Backward Transition: 

1. From Parallel Shunt to Parallel. 

a. LS3 drops out due to decreased output of axle generator. This unshunts the motor fields. 

2. From Parallel to Series-Parallel Shunt. 

a. LS2 drops out due to decrease in out put of axle generator causing a backward transition. 

3. From Series Parallel Shunt to Series Parallel. 

a. LS1 drops out due to decrease in output of axle generator and unshunts the motor fields.

SECTION 6 AIR SYSTEM Description: 

Every road locomotive unit is equipped with a Westinghouse Type 3CDB three cylinder air cooled compound type air compressor. It is driven from the Diesel engine crankshaft through a flexible coupling.

Draining Main Reservoirs: 

The main reservoirs should be drained every crew change to prevent moisture from reaching the air brake and control air systems.

The governor oil system as shown in Plate 2700-11 consists of a reservoir, strainer, pump, filter and low oil pressure switch. 

The system operates on 125 psi. oil pressure. A motor driven pump receives oil from the reservoir through a strainer and passes it through a filter to the governor at high pressure.

There are two types of reservoirs in service. One uses the bayonet gauge for determining oil level and the other has a sight level glass. (See Plate 2700-12).

DIESEL ELECTRIC PASSENGER LOCOMOTIVES CLASS 90 

PART II-SECTION 8 

90

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

The 90 class locomotive was built by the Fairbanks- Morse & Company with General Electric Company for passenger service. The locomotive is powered by a F-M 10 cylinder, 2000 hp., opposed piston, Model 38 D Diesel engine.

The en- gine speeds are controlled by the electro-hydraulic General 

Electric governor, Model 17 MG. The generator is mounted directly to the engine crankshaft through a flexible coupling. The auxiliary generators and amplidyne exciter are mounted to the generator and are gear driven from the free end of the generator. One auxiliary generator furnishes power for cooling fans and traction motor blowers, and the other furnishes power for the low voltage system. The third auxiliary generator is known as the amplidyne exciter and furnishes the excitation for the main generator. 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION 

This locomotive must not be operated in excess of 100 m.p.h. The overspeed control switch which is set at 100 m.p.h. will set the air brakes if this speed is exceeded. 

The control station contains the necessary equipment and controls for operation of the locomotive. (See Plate 2800-2). 

The transition meter shown at the control station is an ammeter in the No. 1 traction motor circuit and indicates the current flowing through that traction motor. 

If for any reason the No. 1 traction motor is cut out, the transition meter will be inoperative. 

The "PC" switch is of the automatic reset type and will return the engines to idle when tripped. To recover the control of the locomotive, return the throttle to idle until the brake application has been recovered. The "PC" switch will operate on any safety device pedal, automatic train control, automatic train stop, locomotive overspeed or any emergency brake application. 

The controls at the left side of cab are the steam generator controls as shown in Plate 2800-1.

The hand brake lever is located in the nose compartment of the lead units and is adjacent to the water tank at the generator end of the booster unit.

Engine Control & Instrument Panel: 

This panel is located on the car body wall adjacent to the left side of the engine, which is the right side of the locomotive. 

This panel contains all switches necessary to start and prepare engine for operation. The engine control knob, similar to the isolation switch on E.M.D. locomotives in function, is also used to start the engines. This is done by

To Start Engines: 

(a) Check engine overspeed governor reset lever. 

To reset, pull reset lever in direction indicated on the name plate. The lever must be pulled against heavy spring pressure. 

Handle will be approximately horizontal when governor is reset. (b) Snap ON fuel pump switch at engine control panel. 

Fuel pressure should build up to 20-30 psi. (c) Move engine control (E.C.) switch to IDLE position. 

Then after pausing an instant, pull out on E.C. switch. which is the START position. Lube oil (d) Hold knob in START position until engine starts and low oil pressure alarm light goes out. pressure should build up to 8 psi. (e) If engine does not start, repeat the starting procedure. DO NOT ATTEMPT to start engine repeatedly as this will run down the storage batteries. 

After Engines Start: 

Should be at least 8 psi. (a) Check lube oil pressure. (b) See that air compressor and traction motor blowers are operating. (c) See that radiator shutters are in their normal position. (d) Check battery voltage, should be approximately 75 volts. (e) Put engines on the line by moving engine control switch to the RUN position, pausing 3 seconds in each position.

To Move Locomotive: 

(a) See that air brake equipment is properly set. 

(b) PLACE FOOT ON safety device pedal before releas ing locomotive air brakes. 

(c) Close GENERATOR FIELD AND CONTROL circuit breaker on control stand panel. (d) Release brakes and open throttle as required.

Operation of Transition Lever: 

This locomotive is equipped with automatic transition and will make transition automatically both forward and

1. Engine Tachometer 2. Ground Relay Tripped Indicating Light (white) 3. Boiler Flame Out Indicating Light (blue) 4. Lube Oil Pressure Gage 5. Low Oil Pressure Indicating Light (yellow) 6. Fuel Oil Pressure Gage 7. Hot Engine Indicating Light (red) 8. Engine Control Switch 9. Fuel Pump Switch PLATE 2800-3 ENGINE CONTROL PANEL

backward as long as the transition lever is kept in the No. 4 position.

Automatic transition is made on the basis of locomotive speed so is NOT dependent on main generator voltage or amperage in making transition as are the E.M.D. locomotives.

To hold the locomotive in any transition position, move the lever to that position. The locomotive will make transition automatically to that position but not beyond. For instance, if the transition lever is placed in the No. 2 position, the locomotive will make transition from 1 to 2 and 2 to 1 automatically. 

Transition will normally take place automatically.

1 to 2 2 to 3 3 to 4 25.5 m. p.h. 32 m.p.h. 64 m.p.h. 

When operating on grades that level off slightly, causing the locomotive to make transition forward one step and then return almost immediately, place the transition lever in the position at which the locomotive operates a major portion of the time. This will forestall undesired forward transition. 

Load Meter Indication: 

The locomotive Load Meter is graduated in 100 amp. increments and has a scale from 0 to 1800 amps. The load meter has white, yellow and red sections to enable the operator to easily read the indicated reading. See Plate 2800-4.

As long as the indicator pointer is in the white area, the locomotive is not overloaded but does become overloaded when the pointer reaches 1200 amps. or is in the yellow area 

90 minutes. The table below indicates the maximum allowable time that the locomotive may be operated in an overloaded condition. These ratings are not accumulative. 20 m.p.h. for 30 min. or 10 miles at 1200 amps. 

17 m. p.h. for 15 min. or 4.2 miles at 1300 amps. 

14 m. p.h. for 10 minutes or 2.2 miles at 1400 amps. 

11 m.p.h. for 6 minutes or 1.0 mile at 1500 amps. 

9 m.p.h. for 4 minutes or 0.6 miles at 1600 amps.

The short time or overload ratings must not be exceeded under any circumstances.

If, for instance, the pointer levels off at 1250 amps., the locomotive can be operated for 15 minutes or 4.2 miles at this rating. After any one of the above ratings has been used, the load on the locomotive must be reduced a sufficient amount so that the pointer moves out of the overload area for at least 15 minutes before again entering the overload area.

Whenever any of the power wheels slip on the locomotive, the indicator on the instrument panel will light. When wheel slippage occurs, sand should be used and throttle should not be reduced. The wheel slip device automatically reduces the power on the unit on which the wheel slippage occurs and eliminates the need for reducing the throttle. 

The wheel slip light will not burn continuously because wheels do not slip continuously. The connections from the wheel slip relay to the power plant are such that the power on the unit is automatically reduced when the wheels slip. 

Therefore, wheels will slip, then stop, slip again, and stop about once a second.

A steady glow of the wheel slip light indicates electrical trouble and should be investigated immediately. 

Air Braking With Power: 

When it is necessary with full power applied to the locomotive, the throttle should be reduced to at least the sixth notch. It is imperative that locomotive brakes be released and held in the release position when the locomotive is supplying power.

When preparing to stop with power applied to the locomotive and brakes applied to the train, reduce the throttle setting as the train speed drops. The throttle should be in idle before the locomotive stops. This is because the pulling power (tractive effort) of the locomotive greatly increases as the speed drops.

These instructions are not to interfere with the operating instructions covering the use of dynamic or air brakes. 

Emergency Stop Button: 

The red button on the control stand in the cab of the locomotive is to be used to shut down all engines of the locomotive in case of emergency.

This shuts the engines down in such a manner that all engines must be isolated before any engine can again be started. This is done by turning each engine control switch to the idle position. Start engines in the usual manner. Dynamic Brake: The elec- The dynamic brake is an electrical retarding device This which utilizes the traction motors as generators. action creates a retarding force on the train. trical energy generated by the traction motors is dissipated as heat by the dynamic brake grids.

To apply the dynamic brake, first close the throttle lever to idle, after waiting 10 seconds move the transition selector handle to the off position. At this position, the load-braking pointer should show a slight movement. Disengage the transition selector handle and move it slowly into the "B" position. Stop this movement when the pointer reaches 100 amps. This should produce enough braking effort to bunch the slack of the train. When slack is bunched, apply the brake as needed within the limits of the locomotive. 

The 90 class locomotive has 900 amp. dynamic braking limit. This is shown on the load meter as the point at which the red starts on the braking band.

If it is impossible to maintain desired train speed by use of the dynamic brake only, it is permissible to use the train brakes in conjunction with the dynamic brake. However, be sure to keep locomotive brakes RELEASED AT ALL TIMES 

when using the dynamic brake.

Below 27 m.p.h. the available braking effort of the locomotive diminishes. Therefore, when coming to a stop, it is necessary to release the dynamic brake and apply the independent brake sufficiently to prevent a run-out of the locomotive. Complete the stop in the prescribed manner. 

Differences in idling speeds of the engines of the locomotive may cause the warning light to come on before maximum allowable braking effort is reached. At the first indication of overload, reduce the dynamic braking effort. Failure to do so will result in damage to the grids or traction motors. 

DO NOT PLACE AN ENGINE on the line while in dynamic braking. 

The dynamic brake on any unit can be cut-out by moving the engine control switch to the idle position. THIS SHOULD 

NOT BE DONE, except in cases of extreme emergency, while the dynamic brake is in use.

To avoid surges on the equipment and on the train, the dynamic brakes should not be cut in or out on any unit except when the selector handle (transition lever) is in the OFF position.

A dynamic brake interlock is provided between the locomotive air brakes and the dynamic brake. This interlock prevents the application of the locomotive brakes when an automatic brake application is made while the dynamic brake is in When an emergency application occurs from any cause, the interlock is nullified and full air pressure is applied to the locomotive brake cylinders. use.

When using the dynamic brake and an emergency application is made, return the selector handle to the OFF position immediately.

Operating With Steam Locomotive Helper: 

Steam locomotive tonnage ratings are often based on speeds lower than the continuous rating speed of the Diesel. 

It is therefore necessary to be careful that the Diesel locomotive does not pull more than its share of the tonnage and overheat the traction motors. The Diesel locomotive may be operated at speeds at or above 23 m.p.h. at full throttle.

If the grade and/or tonnage are so great that the locomotive will not maintain this speed, it will be necessary to reduce the throttle according to the chart shown below:

Throttle 8765 m.p.h. 23 & up 18 to 23

Towing Locomotive: 

When the locomotive is to be moved dead in train or towed by other power in yard movements, place the throttle in the idle position and move the reverser handle to the OFF position and remove the handle from the control stand. 

If the locomotive is moved dead in train, take the following action:

Lead Units: 

(a) Close brake pipe cut-out cock and remove automatic brake valve handle in running position. (b) Place dead engine cock in Dead position on D-24 brake control valve. (c) Place charging change over cock in Passenger position on D-24 control valve. (d) Remove or keep independent brake valve handle in 

Release position. (e) Place Rotair valve handle in Passenger position.

Booster Units: 

(a) Place dead engine cock in Dead position on D-24 control valve. (b) Open brake valve cut-out cock (hostler's station).

Remove brake valve handle in Full Application po sition, then move brake valve stem to Release position, using Reverse side of brake valve handle. (d) Place charging change-over and control emergency cocks in Passenger position. (e) Close reducing valve cutout cock. (f) Open end cocks on actuating and application release pipes on end of unit. 

If the locomotive or unit is to be moved dead in train during freezing weather, drain the engine cooling system and steam generator of water to prevent freezing of the equipment.

Isolating an Engine While Under Power: 

To isolate an engine while under power, turn the E.C. (engine control) switch, located on the engine control panel, to the idle position. In doing this, pause at No. 6, No. 4 and No. 2 throttle positions to allow time for the engine to unload and reduce speed gradually. If the engine is to be shut down, follow the instructions given under "Stopping an Engine. 

Precautions During Locomotive Operation: 

(a) Maintain engine cooling water temperature from 

150° to 180° F., preferably at 1650 F. Do not exceed 200° F. Engines are equipped with automatic shutter controls which open the shutters at 1550 F.

If for any reason the automatic shutters become inoperative, they can be operated manually. Be sure that engine cooling water temperature is up to 1250 F. before engine is worked. (b) Lube oil pressure should be 20 to 35 psi. in Run 8. 

At idling speed the lube oil pressure should be above 8 psi. The oil pressure switch will shut the engines down if the oil pressure falls to 5 psi.

In Run 8, the minimum lube oil pressure is 18 psi. (c) The desired engine cooling water level to be maintained is not less than 3/4 glass in roof tank sight glass. See Section 2 on Cooling System. 

(d) Charging ammeter should indicate charge at all times and auxiliary generator voltage should be approximately 75 volts. (e) Control air pressure should be approximately 80 psi. (f) With engine running, the lube oil level should be within 1/2 in. of the high mark on bayonet gage in the side of the crankcase. (g) If a circuit breaker should trip, the handle will be midway between ON and OFF. To reset, move handle to OFF position and then snap ON. In some cases it may be necessary to wait a few minutes before resetting the circuit breaker. 

Stopping Engines: 

1. Move engine control switch to idle. 

2. Stop engine by moving engine control switch to OFF. 

3. Turn OFF fuel pump switch.

If locomotive is to be shut down completely: 

(a) Open all switches on Control Stand and at Control Panel on Control Compartment. 

(b) Open main battery.

TROUBLE SHOOTING If Fuel Pump Does Not Run: 

(a) Check all circuit breakers in circuit. (1) On control stand. (2) On control compartment panel. (3) On engine control panel.

If Fuel Pump Runs & Engine Does Not Receive Sufficient Fuel: 

(a) Check fuel supply and emergency fuel cut-off valve.

Pressed. (a) Check to see that fuel pump is running and fuel pressure gauge shows 12 to 18 psi. (b) Check engine overspeed reset lever located on left side of engine over governor.

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle is Opened: 

(a) Check to see that brakes are released. (b) Check to see that generator field switch at control stand is closed. (c) Check to see that ground protective relays are set in normal position and engine control switches are in "RUN" position. (a) Check following circuit breakers: (1) Auxiliary generator field. (2) Auxiliary generator.

If an Engine Fails to Stop: 

If the engine does not stop when the engine control switch is moved to the "OFF" position, turn fuel pump switch to the "OFF" position on the engine control panel. 

Traction Motor Blowers: 

In the event of a traction motor blower failing, serious damage could result from traction motor overheating. (1) When a traction motor blower stops, the engine will go to idle and the alarm bell will ring on all units and the Traction Motor Blower and Brake

Resistor Warning Lights will light on the units on which failure occurs. (2) In case of traction motor blower failure, check 300 amp. auxiliary generator fuse located in left side of high voltage cabinet. DO NOT REMOVE OR
