Shunt traction motor fields to increase motor speed.

Controlled by field shunt relays. 

Nullifying Switch (Separate Pressure Switch on Older Units (Null Sw.): 

Contact in throttle closing at approximately 650 engine

RPM to nullify low power control. 

Overriding Solenoid (in Governor) (ORS): 

Prevents load regulator from going to maximum field during removal of excitation or cutting out of traction notors. 

Power Contactors (P1, P2): 

Connects traction motors to the main generator.

Reverse Current Relay (RC): 

Opens Battery Charging Contactor (BC) when battery voltage exceeds auxiliary generator voltage. This prevents current from the auxiliary generator to the battery from reversing. 

Radiator Fan Contactors (RF1, RF2, RF3): 

Control radiator cooling fan motor speeds.

Fan-Shutter Control Switches (S1-S4, S11-S44): 

Control SMV and fan contactors. 

Shutter Magnet Valve (SMV): 

Controls main reservoir air to the top shutter operating cylinder. 

Signal Relay (SR): 

Energizes alarm bells.

Traction Motor Cutout Switch (TMCO): 

Knife or selector switch to cut out traction motors on either truck by de-energizing contactor Pl or P2. P contactor interlocks energize ORS to insert resistance in exciter battery field to reduce main generator output 50%.

Throttle Pressure Switch (Termed EPS on some units) (TPS): 

Closes when main reservoir air pressure drops to 80 lbs.

, to energize TLV. 

Throttle Line Magnet Valve: (TLV) : 

When energized by TPS, closes off air in throttle line to give throttle control in event of loss of main reservoir air pressure.

Throttle Magnet Valve (pneumatic throttle control)(TV): 

When de-energized, vents governor air line to bring engine speed to idle.

Voltage Regulator (VR): 

Regulates auxiliary generator voltage. Correct settings are 72 volts idling. 75 volts full speed.

Wheel Slip Relays (WS1, WS2): 

Sound buzzer and light warning light at control station when wheels slip on any unit; also automatically cut off power on unit affected by de-energizing contactor EF. 

Operation of relays and contactors during transition: 

Control sequence is described as follows: 

1. Near maximum generator volts, voltage relay FS-1 picks up. 

2. The first two "M" shunt contactors close. 

3. As locomotive speed increases further, main generator voltage again nears the maximum, and voltage relay FS-2 energizes. 

4. The second two "M" shunt contactors close. 

5. With the locomotive operating in the second step of shunting, assume that speed lessens due to a grade, raising generator current and lowering generator voltage. At approximately 1000 generator amperes, FS-2 drops out.

Gener- 

6. The second two "M" shunt contactors drop out. ator voltage increases and current decreases. 

7. If speed lessons further, generator current again rises, and FS-1 drops out. 

8. The first two "M" shunt contactors drop out. The locomotive is now operating in full field at about 11 MPH.

SECTION 6 AIR SYSTEM Description: 

Each locomotive is equipped with a Westinghouse 3CD air compressor to furnish air for locomotive operation and air brake system. This compressor is connected to the main crankshaft through the main generator armature by means of a flexible coupling. 

The main reservoirs are located about the middle of the locomotive underneath the car body. 

In order to prevent moisture from being carried into the air brake system and control air system. These reservoirs should be drained at each crew change.

Santa Fe 

DIESEL ELECTRIC ALCO LOCOMOTIVE CLASS 2100 SUPPLEMENT FOR 2099 

PART II--SECTION 14 

2103

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

The 2100 class locomotive is built by Alco-G. E. and is classified as a 1600 HP. 6 motor Road Switcher. It is a heavy duty single unit locomotive designed for, freight, passenger, and switching service. Controls are applied for multiple unit operation of two, three, or four units controlled from one cab. The locomotive is powered by a 12 cylinder V type 9" x 10" single acting turbosupercharged, 1600 HP. diesel engine. (See Section 10 part 1), The locomo tive is equipped with automatic transition. The locomotive uses the G. E. electric governor. 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION 

This locomotive must not be operated in excess of 65 m.p.h. 

The control station contains the necessary controls for operation of the locomotive. (See Plate 3400-1).

The load meter shown at the control station, is an ammeter in the traction motor circuit, and indicates the amperage flowing to each traction motor. The load meter is strictly a color band guide for locomotive operation. 

The green zone represents normal operation, in this zone operating time is unrestricted. The yellow zone indicates short time load capacity of the traction motors, operation in this zone is principally for acceleration. The point on this scale where the color band changes from Green to Yellow indicates the maximum load at which continuous operation may be maintained. 

The controls for stopping and starting the diesel engine are located on the engine control panel. This panel is located in the cab.

Starting engines: 

1. Close battery switch. 

2. See that all circuit breakers on the control compartment panel are closed. 

3. Toggle switches on control stand will operate lights. 

4. Move throttle to idle. 

5. Move reverser and selector handles to off. 

6. The ground relay indicator must not show red. If tripped-reset. 

7.

The traction motor cut-out switch pointer should indicate "All MOTORS IN".

8. Close the Control and Fuel Pump Breakers on control stand. Allow pressure to build up to 35-45 lbs. on fuel oil pressure gauge. 

9. Turn Engine Control Switch to idle. Low oil pressure green light will light and alarm bell will ring until engine is started and oil pressure closes the pressure switch. 

10. Turn Start Switch clockwise to crank engine. Hold in this position while cranking the engine until oil pressure shows on gauge, alarm stops and green light goes out. WARNING: Releasing the Start Switch before the green light goes out will stop the engine. If this happens, engine MUST be allowed to come to a complete STOP before repeating the above starting procedure. 

11. Do not discharge battery by repeated attempts to crank. 

If the first two or three attempts are not successful, recheck complete starting preparation. 

12. Check Lubricating Oil Pressing Gauge on Gauge Panel for 20 psi minimum pressure.

13. Open Crankcase Exhauster Circuit Breaker located on Control Compartment Panel 14. Check Engine Lubricating Oil Level. Keep level at the 

HIGH mark on the bayonet gauge with the engine idling, Crankcase Exhauster shut off and the locomotive on a level track. 

15. Close Crankcase Exhauster Circuit Breaker. 

16. Make sure Crankcase Exhauster Light on Control Compartment Panel is ON. 

17. Move Engine Control Switch to Run Position. 

To Move Locomotive: 

1. Close Generator Field Breaker on Control Stand. 

2. Move Reverse Handle to FORWARD or REVERSE position, depending on direction desired. 

3. Move Selector Handle to Position 1 or 2 (or to Posi tion 4 if locomotive is equipped with automatic transition). 

4. Release Brakes. 

5. Open Throttle. 

Operation of Transition Lever and Transition Indicator: 

When starting a train with a Road Switcher equipped with manual transition, move the Selector Handle to Position 1 or 

2. As the speed increases, the pointer on the speed-transition meter will move to zone 3 and then 4 at which time move the Selector Handle to its corresponding positions 3 or 4. 

As locomotive speed decreases move Selector Handle to the corresponding position indicated by the speed-transition meter. 

The Selector Handle may be moved to any motoring position regardless of the Throttle position.

Operation of  

1. Wheel slip is indicated by a warning light and buzzer.

The occasional wheel slip will be corrected by automatic power reduction and smooth reapplication. 

2. Upon repeated slip indication, apply sand. 

3. If this does not correct slipping, reduce throttle. 

4. When a spinning slip develops it cannot be arrested by sand application. Throttle must be reduced until spinning stops, then apply sand and re-apply power. 

5. The loadmeter is another means by which a wheel slip may be detected. A sudden drop of the loadmeter pointer or an abnormally low reading may be an indication of wheel slip. 

6. In multiple-unit operation under adverse rail conditions, excessive speed slip can be avoided by placing

the Engine Control Switch of the lead unit in the number 6 position. Under these conditions, the loadmeter will read low, therefore the minimum continuous speed should be observed. 

7. A continuous wheel slip warning may indicate a locked axle. Check should be made to insure free rotation of all wheels.

Air Braking With Power: 

1. Gradually apply automatic brake for a light brake pipe reduction. 

2. Release locomotive brakes by depressing independent valve handle in the Running position. 

3. Reduce throttle to maintain Loadmeter pointer in Green band of motoring scale as train speed decreases. Move throttle to Idle before train comes to a dead stop. 

4. On locomotives equipped with manual transition move the Selector Handle into the position indicated by the Speed-transition meter. 5. 

Mechanical Interlocking Between Handles: 

1. Throttle Handle. 

(a) Can be moved from IDLE position only with Selector 

Handle in 1, 2, 3, or 4, and Reverse Handle installed. 

2. Selector Handle 

(a) Can be moved from OFF to the 1, 2, 3 or 4 position regardless of Reverse Handle position. (b) On units equipped with dynamic braking the Selector

Handle can be moved into the braking range only when Reverse Handle is FORWARD or REVERSE and throttle Handle is in IDLE: 

3. Reverse Handle. 

(a) Can be moved only when Throttle Handle is in IDLE, and with the Selector Handle in any Motoring position. (b) Can be installed or removed only when in OFF position. 

Towing Locomotive. 

In freezing weather, drain engine and steam generator (if used) water systems. Brake equipment on one or more "dead" units which are in multiple with a "live" leading unit should be set up the same as "live" trailing units, It is recommended that brake equipment on each unit of a "dead" multiple unit locomotive which is not in multiple with a "live" unit should be set up as a single "dead" unit as follows" 

1. Close brake pipe cutout cock on brake valve pedestal- "dead" position if unit is equipped for M-U operation. 

2. Place automatic and independent brake valve handles in "running" position.

3. Place throttle in Idle, selector and reverse handles in Off position-remove reverse handle. 

4. Place dead engine cock in "dead" position. 

5. Set safety valve on distributing valve for 25 psi. 

6. Connect brake pipe hose only.

Taking Diesel Engine "Off the Line" in M-U Operation: 

Turn the Engine Control Switch to Idle position. If it becomes necessary to stop the engine, turn Engine Control Switch to OFF position and open the Fuel Pump Breaker on the Control Compartment Panel. 

Putting Diesel Engine "On the Line" in M-U Operation. 

If engine has been shut down:

1. Close Fuel Pump Breaker on Control Compartment Panel. 

2. Start Engine in usual manner. 

If lead unit throttle is in Idle, turn Engine Control 

Switch directly to Run. 

If lead unit throttle is in a Running Notch, turn Engine

Control Switch slowly to Run pausing 3 seconds at intermediate positions 2, 4 and 6 to gradually bring engine up to speed and load.

Precautions During Locomotive Operation 

1. Engine cooling water temperature should be maintained at 1500 plus or minus 100. The engine water temperature is controlled by a single radiator fan and a pair of radiator shutters located on the right and left sides of the locomotive at the rear end. The speed of the fan and the positioning of the shutters are automatically controlled by the temperature of the water entering the Diesel engine. In the event of high water temperature or by inspection, it. is found that either the fan or shutters or both are not functioning, the equipment can be operated_manually. 

2. Lubricating oil pressure should indicate 20-25 psi at idling speed and 45-55 psi at top engine speed. Engine should not be operated in Run 8 when oil pressure drops below 20 psi. 

3. Do not operate engine if water level is not showing in water tank sight glass. 

4. The oil level should always be between the high and low marks on the bayonet gauge. 

5. The battery charging ammeter may show various degrees of charging but should never show discharge. 

6. Booster air pressure gauge should indicate 15-18 psi with throttle in eighth notch with engine fully loaded and lower pressures in lower throttle notches. 

7. Control air pressure should be 70 psi. 

8. Radiator fan control air pressure, gauge should be 15-17 psi. for operation of electro-pneumatic fan control switches

1. Open Generator Field Breaker located at Control 

Stand. 

2. Move Engine Control Switch to OFF position. 

3. Apply Hand Brake and release Air Brakes. 

4. Open all other Switches and Circuit Breakers at Control 

Stand. 

5. Open Battery Switch. 

Note: Engine Stop Button to be used for Emergency Stop 

ONLY. 

Multiple Unit Operation or Changing Operating Ends: 

1. On Unit Being Cut Out 

(a) Make full service application with automatic brake valve. (b) Place brake pipe cutout cock in Trail position. (c) Move automatic brake valve to Lap and independent to Running and remove handles. (d) Place Reverse Handle in Off position and remove. 

To do this, Selector Handle must be OFF and throttle in Idle. 

(e) On the Control Stand, open the Control and Genera tor Field Breakers leaving the Fuel Pump Breaker closed. 

2. On Unit Being Cut In 

(a) Insert Reverse Handle in controller and leave in 

Off position. (b) Move independent brake valve to Application position. (c) Place brake pipe cutout cock in Lead position. (d) Move automatic brake valve handle to Running position. 

(e) Close Control and Fuel pump breakers on the Control 

Stand. 

(f) Open the Fuel Pump Breaker on the Control Stand ON UNIT BEING CUT OUT: 

(g) Close Generator Field Breaker.

TROUBLE SHOOTING If fuel Oil Pressure Gauge Shows Zero Pressure: 

1. Fuel booster pump not operating. a. Battery switch not closed. b. Control positive and negative circuit breakers in 

OFF position. c. Fuel pump circuit breakers and switch in OFF position. d. Defective oil pressure gauge e. Defective oil booster pump motor f. No fuel. g. Duplex suction filter handle not in vertical or horizontal position.

2. If fuel pump motor is running but no fuel is being delivered. (a) Emergency fuel cut off-valve tripped. (b) Filters dirty or packed too tight. (c) Defective booster pump. (d) Suction pipe broken or leaking. (e) Stuck pressure regulating valve.

1. Repeat customary procedure, checking to make sure all control switches are in the correct position. 

2. Check all circuit breakers in starting circuit. 

3. Check to see if starting contactors come in. 

4. Check batteries by turning on engine room lights and pressing "Start" button. If lights dim out, it is because batteries are weak.

If Engine Rotates But Does Not Fire: 

1. Check engine overspeed trip. 

2. Check fuel pump to make sure fuel is being delivered to engine. 

3. Check injector linkage, it may be stuck in closed position, shutting off the supply of fuel. 

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle is Opened: 

1. See that generator field circuit breaker at engine control stand is closed. 

2. Check hand and air brakes for full release. 

3. Check ground relay and reset if necessary.. 

4. Engine Control switch should be in "run" position. 

5. Check following: 

1. Exciter field circuit breaker. 

2. Auxiliary generator field circuit breaker. 

3. Control circuit breaker. 

To reset Circuit Breakers: 

When a circuit breaker has been tripped, move switch to "OFF" position and then move to "ON" position.

If Engine Does Not Load Up: 

1. Check ground relay. 

2. Starting contactor interlock open. 

3. Wheel slip relay stuck. 

4. Repeated ground relay operation indicates engine should not be operated until trouble is found and corrected. 

If Battery Charging Ammeter Shows Zero: 

1. Auxiliary generator field circuit breaker must be ON. 

2. Battery charging contactor must be closed.

If Locomotive Suddenly Becomes Inoperative: 

1. Check control circuit breaker. 

2. Check generator field circuit breaker. 

3. Check ground relay.

SECTION 2 COOLING SYSTEM Description: 

The engine in each locomotive unit has an individual cooling water system. The cooling water circulated by a pump flows to right and left bank headers which are connected to the water jackets by individual jumpers. 

For automatic operation, the radiator fan control switch should be in "automatic" position on the control panel mounted under the hood on the left back side of the radiator compartment bulkhead. Both shutter motor drive rod yokes should be connected to the shutter operating links with link locking pins removed and placed in receptacle in the inside of the hinged cover. Both cutout cocks should be open and the air pressure gauge should show 15 psi with the shutters closed and the fan not operating. 

In an emergency, the equipment can be operated manually by first closing the cutout cocks to both air filters and then bleed the system by opening the drain valve in the base of each filter. Remove one inch pipe from receptacle on walkway and apply to stub end of shutter operating link. Lift up on pipe and remove chained pin from shutter motor yoke. Place yoke in lockout position and reapply chained pin. Position shutter operating link with locking pin in one of the three holes provided for a full open, mid-position or full closed shutter and fan control switch to "OFF", "Medium" or "Full" fan speed to maintain 1400 to 160° F engine water temperature. 

To Fill Cooling System: 

The system may be either filled under a maximum pressure of 25 psi by connecting a hose to the filler nozzle and opening the filler-drain valve, or by gravity through a roof filling connection into the expansion tank. 

To Drain Cooling System: 

Open the filler-drain valve and the inlet and outlet valves at the cab heater. By opening these valves water will drain from the expansion tank, radiators, oil cooler, cab heater and all of the diesel engine except the lower portion of the volute casing of the water pump. Remove the pipe plug in the bottom of this casing to completely drain the water pump and open cab heater vent valves.

SECTION 3  Description: 

All working parts are pressure lubricated by a gear type pump, gear driven from the engine crank shaft. The engine is classified as a wet sump engine since the lubricating oil is stored in the engine base. The oil is hot and dirty as it has cleaned, cooled and lubricated the various working parts on its course through the engine. There the oil must be again cleaned (by filters) and cooled (by the cooling water) before it is returned to the engine. 

The oil level may be checked, with the engine running, crank case exhauster off, and maintained between the high and low marks on the bayonet gauge. 

Lube Oil Pressure: 

1. If oil pressure drops below 20 psi, but remains above 

7 psi when the Throttle Handle is in the 5th to 8th notches and the Engine Control Switch is in "6" or "Run"; the engine will return to idle, the alarm bell will sound and the low lubricating oil pressure green light on the Control Stand will light. If the throttle is reduced to 4th. notch or below; or the Engine 

Control Switch of the affected unit is turned to "4"; the alarm will stop and the engine will assume load up to the 4th notch. 

2. If oil pressure drops below 7 psi, the engine shuts down, the alarm sounds and the low lubricating oil pressure green light comes on.

SECTION 4 FUEL SYSTEM 

The engine has its own fuel system. The fuel tank is located under the floor and can be filled from either side of the locomotive. 

A fuel oil booster pump draws fuel from the tank and distributes it through the fuel headers. (See Plate 3400-5). 

The fuel is drawn from the tank through the suction filter and is then discharged at 35 to 40 psi. by the pump into the pressure filter, from which it flows to the fuel headers. The pressure regulating valve located at the end of the left bank header controls the pressure in both headers.

Emergency Fuel Cut-Off Valve: (See General Section all loco motives)

Fuel Booster Pump: 

The fuel booster-governor pump assembly consists of an electric motor driving a gear type fuel booster pump through a flexible coupling on one end of its shaft and a gear type governor oil pump through another coupling on the other end of its shaft. The brushes for the motor are located at the fuel pump end of the motor.

This section deals with the description and function of the electric equipment on this class of locomotives. Sequence Relays (AVF-AVS-BVS-CVS-DVS) Commutates resistance in governor speed coil and current limit control circuits.

Battery Contactor (B) 

This contactor connects the battery to the auxiliary generator. 

Motor Cut Out Switch (CO) 

This switch used to cut out traction motors if it becomes necessary. 

Automatic Transition Relays (CR2-CR3) 

These relays control the automatic transition shift from 

No.1 to 2, from No. 2 to 3, from No.3 to 4.

Engine Control Switch (EC) 

This switch controls stop and start of engine and limits engine speed to whatever throttle position switch is set for. 

Exciter Field Relay (EF) 

Energizes transformer to furnish excitation for amplidyne exciter. 

Engine Stop Relay (ESR) 

This relay stops engine by opening the governor clutch coil circuit.

Engine Temperature Switch (ETS) 

This switch idles engine, lights hot engine light and rings alarm.

Fuel Pump Contactor (FPC) 

Energizes motor-operated fuel pump.

Generator Field Contactor (GF) 

Connects main generator field with amplidyne exciter armature. 

Governor Oil Pressure Switch (GOP) 

Shuts engine down if governor oil pressure fails.

Ground Relay (GR) 

This relay removes power, idles engine, lights the warning light and rings the alarm whenever energized by a grounded condition.

Cranking Contactors (GS1-GS2) Connects the starting winding in the main generator to the batteries for starting diesel engine. 

Speed Relays (LS2-LS3) 

Control the automatic-transition relays CR2-CR3 respectively.

Field Shunting Contactors (M1-M2-M3-M4) Connects resistance across traction motor fields.

Oil Pressure Relay (OPR) 

Idles engine, lights warning light, rings alarm bell on low lube oil pressure or hot engine.

Low Lube Oil Pressure Switch (OPS2) 

Shuts engine down if lube oil pressure drops to 15 lbs. in any notch.

Engine Overspeed Switch (OS) 

Shuts engine down when engine RPM reaches 1100.

Parallel Motor Contactors Pl-P2-P21-P22-P31-P32 Connects the traction motors across the main generator.

Exciter Field Rectifier (R1) Supplies DC for amplidyne fields F1-E2.

Reverse Current Relay (RC) Control battery charging contactor. 

Reverser Rev.

Controls the direction of the flow of current to the traction motors. 

Safety Relay (SAR) 

Opens governor clutch coil if speed coil circuit fails.

Shutter Magnet Valve (SMV) 

Controls air admission to shutter system.

Signal Relay (SR) 

Energizes alarm gong circuits.

Series Motor Contactor (S1-S21-831) 

Connects traction motors in series across the main generator. 

Sequence Relay (TDR) 

Delays opening of traction motors contactors and reversing to give time for excitation to be reduced. 

Time Delay Relay (TD) 

Delays opening of power contactors until excitation is removed. Transfer Relay (TR) Opens excitation circuits during transition.

Transition Control Relay (TRP) 

Initiates transition of traction motor connections.

Transformer (T1) Supplies power for field circuit, field limit and current limit control.

Wheel Slip Relays (WS1-WS2-WS3) 

Energizes buzzer, lights warning light if wheels slip.

Voltage Contol Relay (X) 

Regulates voltage output of auxiliary generator.

OPERATION OF RELAYS AND CONTACTORS DURING TRANSITION ON LOCOMOTIVES 2100 TO 2109 INCLUSIVE Forward Transition. 

1. Off to Series-Parallel Transition. 

a. Move reverser handle to desired position. b. Move selector handle to position 4. c. Open throttle to energize control circuit. d. Operating coil of reverser is energized to move reverser to proper position and TDR relay is energized. e. TDR relay closes and picks up S1 and S21 contactors. f. EF and GF relays pick up. 

2. Series-Parallel to Parallel Transition. 

a. LS2 relay is picked up by the output of axle generator. b. LS2 relay picks up CR2 relay. c. CR2 relay picks up TR relay. d. TR relay drops out EF and GF relays and GF relay dropping out picks up P1 and P21 contactors. e. Pl and P21 contactors picking up drops out S1 and $21 contactors. f. S1 and S21 contactors dropping out picks up P2 contactors. g. EF and GF relays picks up thru interlock on P2 contactor. 

3. Parallel to Parallel Shunt Transition. 

a. LS3 relay is picked up by the output of axle generator. b. LS3 relay picks up CR3 relay. c. CR3 relay picks up M1 contactor thru interlock on

TR relay. d. M3, M2 and M4 contactors close thru interlocks on M1, M3 and M2 contactors. 

Backward Transition. 

1. Parallel Shunt to Parallel Transition. 

a. LS3 relay drops out due to decreased output of axle generator. b. LS3 relay drops out CR3 relay. c. CR3 relay drops out M1, M3, M2 and M4 contactors. 

2. Parallel to Serie-Parallel Transition. 

a. LS2 relay drops out due to decreased output of axle generator.

b. LS2 relay drops out CR2 relay. c. CR2 relay drops out TR relay causing backward transition to series-parallel. 

3. Series-Parallel Transition to off. 

a. Close throttle to idle will drop out all relays and contactors. 

OPERATION OF RELAYS AND CONTACTORS DURING TRANSITION ON LOCOMOTIVES 2110 AND UP 

1. Off to Series-Parallel Transition: 

a. Move reverser handle to desired position. b. Move selector handle to position 4. c. Open throttle to energize control circuit. d. Operating coil of reverser is energized to move reverser to proper position and TDR relay is energized. e.

TDR relay closes and picks up S1, S21 and S31 contactors. f. EF and GF relays pick up. 

2. Series-Parallel to Parallel Transition. 

a. TRP relay picks up on main generator voltage. b. TD and TR relays pick up. c. TR relay drops out EF and GF relays and GF relay dropping out picks up P1, P21, and P31 contactors. d. P1, P21 and P31 contactors picking up drops out S1, S21 and 831 contactors. e.

S1, S21 and $31 contactors dropping out picks up P2, P22 and P32 contactors. f. P2 contactor picking up drops out TD relay. g. EF and GF relay picks up thru interlocks on P2 and P32. 

Backward Transition: 

1. Parallel to Series-Parallel Transition: 

a. Traction motor amperage drops out TRP relay causing backward transition to series-parallel. 

2. Series-Parallel Transition to Off: 

a. Close throttle to idle will drop out all relays and contactors.

AIR SYSTEM Description: 

Every Locomotive is equipped with a Westinghouse Type 3CDB three cylinder air cooled compound type air compressor. 

It is driven from the Diesel engine crankshaft through a flexible coupling.

Draining Main Reservoirs: 

The main reservoirs should be drained at each crew change to prevent moisture from reaching the air brake and control air system.

MISCELLANEOUS 

The governor oil system as shown in Plate 3400-6, consists of a reservoir, strainer, pump, filter and low oil pressure switch. 

The system operates on 125 psi. oil pressure. A motor driven pump receives oil from the reservoir through a strainer and passes it through a filter to the governor at high pressure.

There are two types of reservoirs in service. One uses the bayonet gauge for determining oil level and the other has a sight level glass.

SUPPLEMENT FOR 2099 

Diesel locomotive 2099 is similar to the 2100 class in that it has the same car body, diesel engine and electrical equipment. It has a four wheel truck, and manual transition. 

The cab has been equipped with dual controls. This locomotive is also equipped with one Vapor Heating Corp. 4625 steam generator for use in passenger service.

The maximum permissible speed of locomotive 2099 is 65 mph.

Santa Fe 

650 CLASS 600 H. P. E. M. D. 

PART II-SECTION 15 

ATISE

The 650 class of diesel-electric locomotive comprises locomotives 650 to 653 inclusive-manufactured by the Electro- 

Motive Division of General Motors Corporation and are powered by Winton 8-201A engines.

The top speed is 40 miles per hour. The cab is located in the back of the locomotive and the hood extends toward the front.

The diesel engine is mounted in the locomotive with the generator toward the front end of the locomotive and the blower end of the engine toward the cab of the locomotive. 

In front of the diesel engine are located the air compressor and radiator cooling fans.

These locomotives have one 8 cylinder 600 horsepower engine, a direct current generator and four traction motors. 

From the power plant the power is distributed to the traction motors which are mounted on the trucks. The traction motors are geared to the axle through spur gears. 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION Control Station: 

The control station contains the locomotive throttle and reverse lever. The control push button box on the left of controller has fused switches for: (See Plate 3500-3) a. Control b. Exciter field c. Engine starting d. Fuel pump e. Cab heater 15 amp. 15 amp. 15 amp. 15 amp. 10 amp. 

On right cab wall is light control box with fused push buttons for: a. Front headlight dim 15 amp. b. Rear headlight dim 

15 amp. c. Number lights 15 amp. 15 amp. 15 amp. d. Gauge lights e. Cab lights 

Above this box is a 5 position headlight switch.

Instrument Panels: 

1. The instrument panel contains the following indicators: 

brake cylinder and brake pipe air gauge, main reservoir and equalizing reservoir air gauge and engine water temperature gauge. The gauges are indirectly lighted for night operation.

2. The engine instrument panel contains the following: 

a. Fuel pressure gauge. 

b. Main bearing oil pressure gauge. 

c. Control air pressure. 

d. Tachometer.

Low Voltage Cabinet: 

This cabinet contains switches and fuses controlling the battery circuits throughout the locomotive (See Plate 3500-3). It is located on the left side of the locomotive. 

The battery voltmeter is in this cabinet and the battery ammeter is located in cab. 

High Voltage Cabinet: 

This cabinet contains relays and contactors controlling the high voltage circuits throughout the locomotive. It is located on the right side of the locomotive (See Plate 3500-3). 

Starting Engine After Layover: 

1. In low voltage cabinet: a. Be sure all fuses are in place. b. Close main battery switch. 

2. At control station: 

a. Put reverse lever in center position. b. Put throttle in idle position. c. Close control push button switch. d. Close fuel pump switch and watch for pressure on fuel return pressure gauge at engine instrument panel. Return pressure should be 5 to 10 pounds per square inch. e. Press in engine "start" button and hold until engine starts. Do not hold button down for more than 10 seconds at a time. f. Check lubricating oil pressure. This should be 

25 to 40 pounds per square inch. Water temperature should be 125° F. before locomotive is worked. 

To Move Locomotive: 

1. Make certain main reservoir pressure is up. 

2. Release hand brake. 

3. Close exciter field switch at control station. 

4. Place reverse lever in forward or reverse position as required. 

5. Pull back on throttle handle. Movement of the throttle handle should be steady and gradual enough to move load without slipping the wheels: Caution. In moving the locomotive do not pump the throttle handle back and forth as this will open and close the contactors and other electrical equipment which must make or break circuits each time the throttle is shut off or opened. A movement of the throttle handle of

approximately 1/2" will close the throttle switch at the bottom of the throttle shaft, thus closing the control circuit, allowing the power output of the generator to be delivered to the traction motors. 

Towing Locomotive: 

1. Stop engine and drain cooling system if freezing weather will be encountered: 

3. Place reverser handle in "OFF" position and remove handle. 

4. Cut in dead engine cock located below cab floor. 

5. Close double heading cock located below automatic brake valve. 

6. Place handle of automatic and independent brake valve in running position. 

7. Set relief valve on distributing valve at 25 lbs. 

8. See that radiator shutters are closed.

Precautions During Locomotive Operation: 

1. Maintain engine cooling water temperature 150° to 180° preferably 1650 whenever possible. Do not exceed 2000 which is accomplished by proper manipulation of shutter control handle in the cab. If engine cooling water temperature can not be maintained at or below 2000 by reducing the throttle, stop engine and do not operate until cause of excessive temperature is corrected. 

2. Lubricating oil pressure should be approximately 28 pounds at full throttle (750 R. P.M.). If main bearing pressure drops below 15 pounds [at full throttle (750 R.P.M.) hot oil]. stop the engine and to not operate engine until condition is corrected. 

Manual Engine Shut Down: 

The lever is located on the engine instrument panel. It is operated by pulling the button on the end of the shut down rod until engine stops. After engine stops return to run position.

Stopping Engine: 

1. At control station: a. Close throttle to idle position. b. Place reverse lever in neutral position and remove handle. c. Open exciter field switch. d. Pull out on engine shut down rod and hold until engine stops. e. Open control switch. f. Open fuel pump switch.

2. In low voltage cabinet: a. Open main battery switch. 

To Cut Out a Traction Motor: 

1. Cut out No.1 and No. 2 traction motors by moving hand lever to up position. 

2. Cut out No.3 and No. 4 traction motors by moving hand lever to down position. 

Note: With the handle straight out both trucks are cut in for normal operation. Cut out lever is located inside electrical cabinet on right side of locomotive. Warning: 

Have throttle closed and reverser in "OFF" position when cutting out traction motors and do not operate with more than half throttle.

TROUBLE SHOOTING If Fuel Pressure Does not Show on Gauge in Cab: 

1. If fuel pump does not run: a. Check all fuses in circuit. (1) 70 amp. fuse in low voltage cabinet. (2) Fuel pump fuse (15 amp.) at control station. b. Check electric connector at fuel pump motor. 

2. If motor is running but no fuel is being delivered: a. Check pump couplings and shaft. b. Check fuel supply and emergency fuel cut-off valve. c. Check for air leaks in suction line due to broken pipe or loose connection. d. Turn handle of fuel purolator below fuel pump several turns. e. Place duplex fuel filter handle in vertical position on filter at fuel pump. 

If Engine Does Not Rotate When Attempt Is Made to Start: 

1. Check to see the main battery switch is closed, reverse lever on neutral, throttle is closed and control switch is in. 

2. Check all fuses affecting starting circuit. a. Starting fuse. This the 400 amp. fuse in the low voltage cabinet. b. 70 amp. control fuse in the low voltage cabinet. c. 15 amp. control fuse. This fuse is opposite the control push button switch on the controller. d. 15 amp. starting fuse. This fuse is opposite the engine start push button switch on the controller. 

3. Battery may be too weak to turn engine over. 

4. Check to see that both starting contactors go in.

If Engine Rotates But Does Not Fire When Start Button Is 

Pressed; 

1. Check cylinder test valves at each cylinder, making sure that they are closed. 

2. Check injector linkage in event that it may be stuck in shutdown position. 

3. Check fuel pressure and supply. 

4. Check to see that engine shut-down rod is in extreme forward position.

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle Is Opened: 

1. Check hand brake. 

2. Repeat movement of brake valve to release brakes. 

3. Check to see that exciter field switch at control station is closed. 

4. Check control air pressure and see that reverser is in proper position. 

5. Check all fuses. a. 70 ampere control fuse in low voltage cabinet b. 15 ampere control fuse opposite control switch on controller. c. 15 ampere exciter field contactors might be open or making poor contact. 

6. If starting contactors stick shut the generator will not deliver power. 

7. The generator or exciter field contactors might be open or making poor contact.

1. Check all fuses in circuit affected. a. 30 ampere auxiliary generator field fuse in the low voltage cabinet. b. 100 ampere battery charging fuse in the low voltage cabinet. 

2. Auxiliary generator drive belts might be loose or broken. 

If Engine Fails to Stop. (by pulling engine shut down rod): 

1. Pull governor control linkage on the engine to "OFF" position manually, holding until engine stops, or 

2. Shut off fuel pump by opening fuel pump switch or 

3. Trip emergency fuel valve, located beside control stand and on each side of locomotive near center. 

If Locomotive Stops in Operation: 

If locomotive suddenly becomes inoperative, immediately check the control fuse (70 amperes) in the low voltage cabinet, then check the control fuse in the box on the controller. 

A wire may have jarred loose or may be burned off. The fuel supply may have stopped.

SECTION 2 COOLING SYSTEM Description: 

Water is drawn from the oil cooler by a centrifugal water pump located on the left side of the engine. This pump circulates water to the bottom of each cylinder liner up through the cored passages of the cylinder liner and cylinder head and out through the outlet manifolds. From the engine, the water flows to two groups of radiator sections located under the roof of the locomotive body. Here the water is cooled returning to the oil cooler.

Filling Cooling System : 

The cooling system contains approximately 150 gallons and may be filled through the filler pipe extension on the roof. (Do not put water in the exhaust stacks.) Fill the cooling system until water runs out the filler pipe. It is best to have the engine idling when system is being filled, to remove air pockets.

Add water very slowly if engine is hot and then only when engine is idling. Do not add cold water to a hot engine that has just been drained as this will crack and warp cylinder heads and liners.

Specially treated water should be used in the cooling system to offset the accumulation of scale or foreign matter which contributes to pitting and overheating the engine. 

The water level should be maintained above the minimum water level mark (3/4 water glass) on water glass in cab and the engine should not be operated with water below this level. 

To Drain Cooling System: 

All that is necessary to drain the cooling system is to open the water drain valve located right behind the oil cooler on the engine room floor. 

SECTION 3 Lubricating Oil System Description: 

The lubricating oil is stored in the lube oil supply tank, instead of the engine sump which classifies the engine as a dry sump type. The system is a pressure system using a positive displacement pump. The oil supply to this pump is drawn through a strainer in the bottom of the supply tank then through a suction pipe to the pump.

A scavenging oil pump is used to draw oil from the engine sump then pump it through the oil cooler to the lube oil filter and return it to the supply tank. (See Plate 3500-2) 

Oil may be added by removing cover on supply tank and pouring required amount through strainer basket. With the engine idling, the oil level should always be between the "high" and "low" marks on the bayonet gauge in the supply tank (preferably at the "halfway" mark).

When starting a cold engine, allow it to idle for some time to see that pressure starts to build up almost immediately. 

The pressure should rise on a cold engine to about 40 lbs. and will run approximately 28 pounds at full throttle (750 

RPM) with engine warm. At idle, pressure should be at least 

15 pounds.

With normal operation, the lubricating oil pressure should not drop below 15 lbs. per square inch with engine running full throttle (750 RPM). 

If oil pressure drops below 15 lbs. with full throttle, stop the engine, and do not operate until condition is corrected. 

Low Oil Pressure Shut Down: 

If the lubricating oil pressure should fail the engine will be returned to idle speed by a low oil pressure shutdown device, and opening the throttle will have no effect on engine speed. This is accomplished by a cylinder containing a piston attached through a linkage to the throttle shaft. 

One side of the piston is subjected to engine oil pressure and the other side to mechanical spring pressure. When the force of the spring overcomes the force of the oil, the piston moves backward closing the throttle shaft to idle position. A spring loaded rod in the throttle linkage permits this action regardless of the position of the throttle. 

Throttle will start to close at 18 lbs. and engine speed will be reduced to idle when pressure drops to 9 lbs.

A fuel tank is mounted under the center of the unit with a capacity of 600 gallons. At the bottom of the tank is a sump from which the fuel pump draws fuel oil through the suction line and suction filter. Fuel can only be drawn from supply sump if the emergency cut-off valve is open. After leaving the pump, fuel is pumped through the duplex Nugent

Emergency Fuel Cut Off Valve (See General Section All Loco motives)

Fuel Pump: 

The fuel pump assembly consists of an electric motor and pump of the rotor gear type. The gear pump is driven by the motor shaft through a flexible coupling. Do not leave the fuel pump running unnecessarily. 

 Battery Charging Contactor: 

(BC) This contactor connects the auxiliary generator to the battery. The reverse current relay closes the battery charging contactor when the auxiliary generator voltage is high enough to charge the battery. 

Power Switch: 

The power switch is an electro-pneumatically operated mechanical device controlled by transition and current limit relays to provide automatic transition of power contactors. 

Voltage Regulator: (VR) 

The voltage regulator maintains the auxiliary generator voltage at a constant of 74 VOLTS. This keeps the battery charged and provides a constant source of low voltage power. 

A defective voltage regulator will be evidenced by excessive fluctuation of the battery charging ammeter. 

Reverse Current Relay: (RCR) 

This relay prevents the battery current from "motoring" the auxiliary generator by opening the battery charging contactor when the auxiliary generator voltage drops below the battery voltage.

Main Battery Switch: 

This switch is in the low voltage cabinet which is located along the center of the left side of the engineroom compartment and isolates all circuits from the battery. Switch should be open when locomotive is standing with engine shut down.

Transition Relay: (VI): 

The transition relay operates on main generator voltage to change the traction motor connections as locomotive speeds and loads change.

Exciter Field Contactor: (EF) 

This contactor connects the battery to exciter to energize exciter generator field. It is located in top of low voltage cabinet. 

Generator Field Contactor: (SH) 

This contactor connects the main generator shunt field to the exciter. It is located on the power switch. 

Current Limit Relay (CLR): 

This is a protective relay to automatically drop engine back to series when a heavy load is put on locomotive while the power switch is in series-parallel position. 

Engine Starting Contactors: (ST+, ST-) 

The starting contactors are used to connect the battery to the main generator for starting the engine. These starting contactors are of the magnetic type and close when the "start" button at control stand is pressed.

Operation of Relays and Contactors During Transitions Between: 

Series . 

Reduced-field (Shunt).

Off To Series: 

. Low Speeds . Intermediate Speeds .High Speeds 

1. Move reverser to forward or reverse position. 

2. Move throttle open to energize control circuit. 

3. Exciter field contactor goes in. 

4. Operating coil of reverser is energized-Reverser moves to forward or reverse position. 

5. Reverser moves to energize power switch operating coils. 

6. Power switch moves to de-energize reverser operating coil, and closes generator field and series contactors. 

Series to Parallel: 

1. The transition relay, operated from "C" interlock of power switch, is energized as the generator speed and voltage is increased. 

2. When transition relay picks up it de-energizes "OFF" coil of power switch to permit it to move forward.

3. Moving forward the series and generator field contactors drop out and Pl contactor goes in. 

4. Transition relay drops out. 5. P2 contactor goes in. 

6. Generator field closes.

Parallel to Shunt: 

1. The transition relay is again operated due to increased voltage of main generator. 

2. Transition relay picking up again permits power switch to move forward. 

3. Motor field shunting contactors close. 

4. The main generator voltage on transition relay remains high and holds power switch in advanced position keeping the parallel, generator field and shunting contactors closed.

Backward Transition: 

Shunt-Parallel: 

1. When train speed reduces, generator voltage drops to release transition relay. 

2. Power switch coils are de-energized and power switch turns backwards to drop out motor field shunting contactors. 

3. Parallel and generator field contactors remain in. 

Parallel to Series: 

1. Transition relay remains out and when speed reaches point for transition, it should be made manually (about 5 MPH). 

2. Drop throttle to idle for 3 seconds. 

3. Power switch moves backwards to off position. 

4. Open throttle and power circuit is energized. 

5. Sequence of "OFF" to "Series" is repeated.

Draining Main Reservoirs: 

SECTION 6 AIR SYSTEM 

The main air reservoirs are located underneath the carbody, about mid-way between the trucks, one on each side of the locomotive. 

To prevent moisture from being carried over into the air brake and control air systems these reservoirs should be drained at each crew change.

Santa Fe 2201 CLASS 1000 H. P. BALDWIN 

PART II-SECTION 16

The 2201 class of diesel-electric locomotive comprises locomotives 2201 to 2206 inclusive manufactured by the 

Baldwin Locomotive Works and are powered by DeLa Vergne Model VO engines (See Part 1, Section 7). 

The cab is located in the back of the locomotive and the hood extends toward the front. 

The diesel engine is mounted in the locomotive with the generator in front of the cab and the front or pump end of the engine toward the front of the locomotive. In front of the diesel engine are located the traction motor blower, radiator cooling fan, cooling water radiator and oil cooling radiator. 

These locomotives have one 8 cylinder 1000 horsepower engine, a direct current generator and four traction motors. 

From the power plant the power is distributed to the traction motors which are mounted on the trucks. The traction motors are geared to the axle through spur gears. 

The engine has a speed range of 265 to 625 RPM which is controlled by the main throttle through an electropneumatic governor control.

Starting the engine is accomplished by pressing the engine start button located on the control stand. This switch energizes the starting contactors, closing the circuit from the batteries to the main generator. The main generator, then operating as a motor, cranks the engine. 

Control Station: 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION 

The control station contains the locomotive throttle and reverse lever. The control push button box on the front of the controller has fused switches for: (See Plate 3600-1) 1. Control 

2. Engine start 15 amp. 15 amp. 

3. Exciter field 15 amp. 15 amp. 15 amp. 

4. Fuel pump 

5. Compressor 

On right cab wall is light control box with fused push buttons for: a. Front headlight - dim 15 amp. b. Rear headlight c. Class light d. Gauge lights dim 15 amp. 15 amp. 15 amp. e. Cab lights 

Above this box is a 5 position 15 amp. headlight switch.

Electrical Cabinet: 

This cabinet contains switches, relays and fuses controlling the electrical circuits throughout the unit. It is located in front of the cab.

Starting Engine: 

1. In electrical cabinet: a. Be sure all fuses are in place. b. Close main battery switch. 

2. At control station: 

a. PUT REVERSE LEVER IN "ENGINE ONLY" POSITION. 

b. Put throttle in idle position. c. Close control push button switch. d. Close fuel pump switch and watch for pressure on fuel pressure gauge at engine instrument panel. Pressure should be 20 to 30 pounds per square inch on engine header gauge. e. Press in engine "start" button and hold until engine starts and lube oil pressure rises to 10

P.S.I. Do not hold button down for more than 

10 seconds at a time.

This f. Check lubricating oil pressure. This should be 35 to 45 pounds per square inch. Water temperature should be 1250 F. before locomotive is worked. g. As soon as engine runs smoothly, press compressor button on control push button box. will start the electrically driven air compressor. Watch main reservoir air pressure gauge to note that the pressure goes to the correct amount (140 pounds) and then stops.

To Move Locomotive: 

1. Make certain main reservoir pressure is up. 

2. Release hand brake. 

3. Close exciter field switch at control station. 

4. Place reverse lever in forward or reverse position as required. 

5. Pull back on throttle handle. Movement of the throttle handle should be steady and gradual enough to move load without slipping the wheels: Caution: In moving the locomotive do not pump the throttle handle back and forth as this will open and close the contactors and other electrical equipment which must make or break circuits each time the throttle is shut off or opened. A movement of the throttle handle of approximately 1/2" will close the control switch, thus closing the control circuit, allowing the power output of the generator to be delivered to the traction motors.

Permissible Amperage: 

The following table shows the amount of current and the time limits over which it may flow: Time Continuous 40 minutes 20 minutes 15 minutes 12 minutes Amperes 1120 1250 1400 1500 1600 At the end of time limit for any value, notch back on the throttle to 1120 amps. or less. Do not again exceed 

1120 amps. for 15 minutes. (Important: Overloading may result in serious damage to the generator and traction motors.)

Engine Overspeed Trip: 

The overspeed trip is mounted adjacent to the governor and is gear driven from the engine camshaft. This device consists of a spring loaded weight which is normally against its inner stop. When the centrifugal force of the weight becomes greater than the spring tension the weight moves outward to release a lever which turns, moving the injector pump control racks to stop position shutting the engine down. 

Towing Locomotive: 

1. Stop engine, close shutters and drain radiating system if freezing weather will be encountered. 

2. Open main battery switch in electrical cabinet. 

3. Remove master controller and reverse lever handles. 

4. Close double heading cock under brake valve. 

5. Cut in dead engine cock at distributing valve below right side of cab. Set relief valve at 25 pounds. 

6. Lock both brake valves in running position. 

Precautions During Locomotive Operation: 

1. Maintain engine cooling water temperature between 

1440 F. and 160° F. This is accomplished by proper operation of the automatic shutters. 

2. Lubricating oil pressure should be approximately 

40 pounds at full throttle (625 RPM). If main bearing pressure drops below 18 pounds at full throttle (625 RPM) with hot oil, stop the engine and do not operate engine until cause of trouble has been corrected. 

3. Turn lube oil purolator handle manually every 2 hrs.

Stopping Engine: 

1. At control station: a. Close throttle to idle position. b. Place reverse lever in neutral position and remove handle. c. Open exciter field switch. d. Open control switch. e. Open fuel pump switch. f. Open compressor switch.

a. Open cylinder test valves. 

3. In electrical cabinet: a. Open main battery switch. 

TROUBLE SHOOTING If Fuel Pressure Does Not Show on Gauge in Cab: 

1. If fuel pump does not run. a. Check fuel pump fuse (15 amp.) in control stand. b. Check 15 amp. control fuse. 

2. If motor is running but no fuel is being delivered: a. Check pump coupling and shaft. b. Check fuel supply and emergency fuel cut-off valve. c. Check for air leaks in suction line due to broken pipe or loose connection. 

1. Check 15 amp. control fuse. This fuse is opposite the control push button switch on the controller. 

2. Battery may be too weak to turn engine over. 

3. Check to see that both starting contactors go in. 

4. Check 15 amp. starting fuse at control stand. 

5. Check to see that throttle is closed and master controller is in "Engine only" position.

If Engine Rotates But Does Not Fire When Start Button is 

Pressed: 

1. Check cylinder test valves at each cylinder, making sure that they are closed. 

2. Check injector linkage in event that it may be stuck in shutdown position. 

3. Check to see if fuel pump is running. 

4. See that overspeed is not tripped.

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle Is Opened: 

1. Check hand brake. 

2. Repeat movement of brake valve to release brakes. 

3. Check to see that exciter field switch at control station is closed and fuse good. 

4. Check control air pressure and see that reverser is in proper position. .Control air pressure should be 

70 P.S.I. in proper position. 

5. Check all fuses. a. 15 Ampere control fuse opposite control switch on controller. b. 15 Ampere exciter field fuse at controller. 

6. The generator or exciter field contactors might be open or making poor contact.

1. Check 80 Ampere battery charging fuse in the electrical cabinet. 

2. Auxiliary generator drive belts might be loose or broken. 

3. Check 15 ampere control fuse at control stand. 

NOTE: If batteries are not charging they must have attention quickly to prevent exhaustion. 

If Air Pressure Does Not Build Up: 

1. Check to see that angle cocks and main reservoir drains are in proper position. 

2. Check main reservoir safety valve in event that it might be stuck open. A light tap may seat it. 

3. Check 350 Ampere compressor fuse in left bottom corner of electrical cabinet.

If Locomotive Stops in Operation: 

If locomotive suddenly becomes inoperative, check the control fuse in the box on the controller. A wire may have jarred loose or may be burned off. The fuel supply may have stopped.

SECTION 2 COOLING SYSTEM Description: 

Water is drawn from the radiators by a centrifugal water pump located on the front end of the engine. This pump circulates water to the engine block where it surrounds the cylinder liners and circulates up through the passages in the cylinder heads and passes into the outlet manifold then back to the radiators.

Filling Cooling System: 

The cooling system contains approximately 320 gallons and may be filled through the filler pipe located along each side under running board near center of locomotive or through the filler pipe extension on the roof. (Do not put water in the exhaust stack.) Fill the cooling system until water overflows. It is best to have the engine idling when system is being filled, to remove air pockets.

Add water very slowly if engine is hot and then only when engine is idling. Do not add cold water to a hot engine that has just been drained as this will crack and warp cylinder heads and liners.

Specially treated water should be used in the cooling system to offset the accumulation of scale or foreign matter which contributes to pitting and overheating the engine. 

The engine should be operated with the water level above

the minimum water level mark (2" FROM BOTTOM OF WATER GLASS) 

and the engine should be SHUT DOWN if water goes below this level. Valve on pipe directly below water glass must be open to get true water level reading. 

Engine Cooling Water Temperature Control: 

1. Thermostatic radiator shutter control. a. The cooling water temperature is controlled by automatic shutters and should maintain the engine temperature between 1440 F. and 1600 F. 

2. Emergency water control. a. If water temperature gauge indicates above 180ºF. check the radiator shutters to see if they are open.

If either shutter is closed, break seal and move corresponding emergency shutter valve to the opposite position.

These valves are located inside engine room behind radiator fan compartment and just inside left front door. b. Check to see that shutter has opened. c. Check water temperature gauge and if temperature remains above 180° F. there is some other cause of overheating and ENGINE SHOULD

BE SHUT DOWN until condition is corrected. 

If the water is to be drained, open drain valve near water pump.

In addition to the drain valve there is a drain plug in the bottom of the water pump.

SECTION 3  Description: 

The lubricating oil is stored in the engine sump which classifies the engine as a wet sump type. The system is a pressure system using a positive displacement pump. The oil supply to pump is drawn through a strainer in the bottom of the sump. 

Oil may be added by removing cover on filler at right front corner of crankcase, and pouring required amount into the engine. With the engine idling, the oil level should always be between the "high" and "low" marks on the bayonet gauge in the right front corner of crankcase (preferably above half way mark).

 When starting a cold engine, allow it to idle for

some time and see that pressure starts to build up immediately.

The pressure should rise on a cold engine to about 45 lbs. and will run approximately 35 pounds at full throttle (625 RPM) with engine warm. At idle, pressure should be at least 15 pounds. With normal operation, the lubricating oil pressure should not drop below 18 lbs. per square inch with engine running full throttle (625 RPM).

IF OIL PRESSURE DROPS BELOW 18 LBS. WITH ENGINE AT FULL THROTTLE (625 RPM), STOP THE ENGINE AND DO NOT OPERATE UNTIL CAUSE OF TROUBLE HAS BEEN CORRECTED. 

Low Oil Pressure Shut Down: 

If the lubricating oil pressure should fail, the engine will be stopped by a low oil pressure shut-down device. (See 

Part 1 Section 7). 

A fuel tank is mounted under the center of the unit with a capacity of 700 gallons. At the bottom of the tank is a sump from which the fuel pump draws fuel oil through the suction filter. Fuel can only be drawn from the supply sump if the emergency cut-off valve is open. After leaving the pump, fuel is pumped through a filter to the fuel injection pumps. 

The fuel injection pumps use only a part of the fuel pumped to them.

Emergency Fuel Cut Off Valve: 

(See General Section all locomotives). 

Fuel Pump: 

The fuel pump assembly consists of an electric motor and pump of the rotor gear type. The gear pump is driven by the motor shaft through a flexible coupling. Fuel pressure should be between 10-25 LBS. at HEADER to fuel injection pumps and 25-35 LBS. at FILTER. If fuel pressure drops below 5 lbs. engine will stop. Do not leave the fuel pump running unnecessarily.

Air Compressor Motor: 

SECTION 5 ELECTRICAL SYSTEM 

This motor drives the air compressor with electrical energy received from the auxiliary generator. 

The ELECTRICAL CABINET in front of cab includes the fol lowing equipment: 

1. Engine Starting Contactors (G1 & G2). 

Establish circuit from batteries to main generator starting field for cranking engine. 

2. Exciter Field Contactor (EF). 

Connects battery to exciter generator field circuit for excitation. 

3. Motor Cut Out Switches (No. 1 & No. 2) 

Switches in circuits to traction motor contactor operating coils used to isolate traction motors two at a time. Open switch on motors to be cut out. 

After cutting motors out, operate with one-half throttle to prevent excessive heating of other traction motors. Do not open cut out switches until the throttle is in idle and reverse lever in engine only positions. 

4. Generator Field Contactor (GF) 

Connects the exciter generator to main generator shunt field. 

5. Field Shunting Relays (FS1, FS2, FS3). 

A voltage relay operating to provide automatic field shunting from parallel to parallel shunt in three successive steps as main generator loads change. 

6. Traction Motor Power Contactors (P1, P2) 

These contactors connect the traction motors to the generator. 

7. Air Compressor Contactor (CA) 

Closes circuit to air compressor motor under control of compressor governor. Air compressor governor stops air compressor when main reservoir pressure reaches 140 lbs. 

8. Field Shunting Contactors (M1, M2, M3, M4, M5, M6)

Connects motor field shunts across traction motor fields. 

9. Compressor governor contactor. 

This contactor controls the air compressor motor according to compressed air requirements. 

10. Voltage Regulator. 

Controls the output of the auxiliary generator at 128 volts regardless of engine speed changes or battery voltage fluctuations. 

11. Battery Charging Contactor (BC). 

Establishes circuit of auxiliary generator to battery charging equipment is controlled by reverse current relay. 

---

12. Reverse Current Relay (RC). 

Protective relay for auxiliary generator it prevents battery current from flowing into auxiliary generator when the auxiliary generator voltage drops below battery voltage. 

13. Main Battery Switch. 

Connects batteries to all locomotive electrical circuits. 

14. Ammeter.

If Ammeter indicates battery charge or discharge. ammeter shows discharge, condition should be corrected immediately as batteries will become exhausted and locomotive will stop. 

These fuses are included in cabinet: 

Auxiliary Generator Fuse-80 amp.

Compressor Fuse-350 amp. 

SECTION 6 AIR SYSTEM Air Compressor: 

Each locomotive is equipped with a 125 Volt, direct current, motor driven Westinghouse 3-Z, water cooled, air compressor of 120 cu.ft. displacement at 870 RPM to supply air for the air brake and control air systems. 

Main reservoir air pressure is maintained between 130 and 140 pounds per square inch and the control air pressure should be 70 pounds per square inch plus or minus 3 pounds. 

Draining the Main Reservoirs: 

Each locomotive has two main air reservoirs located underneath the car body, between the trucks. 

To prevent moisture from being carried over into the air brake and control air systems these reservoirs should be drained at each crew change.

Santa Fe 2207 CLASS 1000 H. P. BALDWIN 

PART II-SECTION 17

The 2207 class of Diesel-electric locomotive comprises Locomotives 2207-2259 manufactured by the Baldwin Locomo tive Works and are powered by DeLaVergne Model VO engines (See Part 1, Section 7). 

The cab is located in the back of the locomotive and the hood extends toward the front. 

The Diesel engine is mounted in the locomotive with the generator in front of the cab and the pump end of the engine toward the front of the locomotive. In front of the Diesel engine are located the traction motor blower, radiator cooling fan, cooling water radiator and oil cooling radiator. (See Plate 3700-2).

These locomotives have one 8-cylinder 1000 hp. engine, a direct current generator and four traction motors. From the power plant the power is distributed to the traction motors which are mounted on the trucks. The traction motors are geared to the axle through spur gears. 

The engine has a speed range of 250 to 625 r.p.m. which is controlled directly by the main throttle through a pneumatic system. These locomotives are equipped with Soft Start characteristics.

Starting the engine is accomplished by pressing the engine start button located on the control stand. This switch energizes the starting contactors, closing the circuit from the batteries to the main generator. The main generator, then operating as a motor, cranks the engine. 

LOCOMOTIVE OPERATION Control Station: 

The control station and instrument panel contains the throttle and reverser. Air brake valves are on a separate pedestal.

Starting Engine: 

1. In electrical cabinet: a. Be sure all fuses are in place. b. Close main battery switch. 

2. At control station: 

a. Put Reverse Lever in Center Position. 

b. Put throttle in idle position. c. Turn control switch to "ON" position. d. Close fuel pump switch and watch for pressure on fuel pressure gauges at engine instrument panel. 

Fuel pressure at pump should be 25 to 35 lb. Fuel pressure at engine should be 10 to 25 lb. e. Press in engine "start" button and hold until engine starts and lube oil pressure rises to 10

P.S.I. Do not hold button down for more than 10 seconds at a time. f. Check lubricating oil pressure. This should be 35 to 45 lb. per square inch. Water temperature should be 1250 F. before locomotive is worked. 

To Move Locomotive: 

1. Make certain main reservoir pressure is up. 

2. Release hand brake.

3. Close exciter field switch at control station. 

4. Place reverse lever in forward or reverse position as required. 

5. Pull back on throttle handle. Movement of the throttle handle should be steady and gradual enough to move load without slipping the wheels. Caution: In moving the locomotive do not pump the throttle handle back and forth as this will open and close the contactors and other electrical equipment which must make or break circuits each time the throttle is shut off or opened. A movement of the throttle handle of approximately 1/2 in. will close the control switch, thus closing the control circuit, allowing the power output of the generator to be delivered to the traction motors.

For 15 Minutes. 

(Important: Overloading May Result In Serious Damage To The Generator and Traction Motors.) Operation of Wheel Slip Buzzer: 

Whenever any pair of wheels slip on either truck the buzzer on the cab instrument panel will sound. To stop the slippage, ease off on the throttle.

The buzzer will not sound continuously when wheels slip because the wheels will not slip continuously. The connection of the wheel slip relay to the power plant are such that power is automatically reduced when the wheels slip. When the wheels stop slipping the power will go on at full strength 

Unless Throttle is Reduced. Unless throttle is reduced the wheels will slip, then stop, slip again and stop. 

If necessary to apply sand reduce the throttle until the wheel slip buzzer stops sounding. It should be remembered that sand is injurious to the traction motors and associated apparatus.

PLATE 3700-4 CAB FRONT-RIGHT SIDE

Engine Overspeed Trip: 

The overspeed trip is mounted adjacent to the governor and is gear driven from the engine camshaft. This device consists of a spring loaded weight which is normally against its inner stop. When the centrifugal force of the weight becomes greater than the spring tension (approximately 690 r.p.m.) the weight moves outward to release a lever which turns, moving the injector pump control racks to stop position shutting the engine down.

Towing Locomotive: 

1. Stop engine, close shutters and drain cooling system if freezing weather will be encountered. 

2. Open main battery switch in electrical cabinet. 

3. Place reverse lever in "OFF" position and remove handle. 

4. Close double heading cock located below brake valve. 

5. Cut in dead engine cock below right side of cab. 

6. Set relief valve on distributing valve at 25 pounds. 

7. Lock both brake valves in running position. 

Precautions During Locomotive Operation: 

1. Maintain engine cooling water temperature between 1440 

F. and 165° F. This is accomplished by proper operation of the automatic shutters. 

2. Lubricating oil pressure should be approximately 40 lb. at full throttle (625 r.p.m.). If main bearing pressure drops below 18 lb. (at 625 r. p.m. hot oil), stop the engine and investigate. 

3. Turn lube oil purolator handle manually every 2 hrs.

1. At control station: 

a. Close throttle to idle position. b. Place reverse lever in neutral position and remove handle. c. Open exciter field switch. d. Turn control switch to "OFF" position. 

2. In electrical cabinet: a. Open main battery switch.

TROUBLE SHOOTING If Fuel Pressure Does Not Show on Gauge in Cab: 

1. If fuel pump does not run, check 25 amp. control fuse in left bottom corner of electrical cabinet. 

2. If motor is running but no fuel is being delivered: a. Check pump couplings and shaft. b. Check fuel supply and emergency fuel cut-off valve. c.

Check for air leaks in suction line due to broken pipe or loose connection.

2. Battery may be too weak to turn engine over.

If Engine Rotates But Does Not Fire When Start Button is 

Pressed: 

1. Check cylinder test valves at each cylinder, making sure that they are closed. 

2. Check injector linkage in event that it may be stuck in shutdown position. 

3. Check to see that overspeed trip is latched in "Run" position. 

4. Check fuel pressure.

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle is Opened: 

1. Check hand brake. 

2. Repeat movement of brake valve to release brakes. 

3. Check to see that exciter field switch at control station is closed. 

4. Check control air pressure and see that reverser is in proper position, control air pressure should be 70 P.S.I. 

5. Check 25 amp. fuse in electrical cabinet (on 2207 check 15 amp. control fuse and 15 amp. exciter field fuse at control station). 

6. The generator or exciter field contactors might be open or making poor contact. 

7. If starting contactors stick shut the generator will not deliver power.

1. Check 100 amp. battery charging fuse in the electrical cabinet. 

2. Auxiliary generator drive belts might be loose or broken. 

3. Check to see that exciter field switch is turned on. 

NOTE: If batteries are not charging they must have attention quickly to prevent exhaustion. 

If Locomotive Stops in Operation: 

If locomotive suddenly becomes inoperative, immediately check the control fuse (25 amp.) in the electrical cabinet.

A wire may have jarred loose or may be burned off. The fuel supply may have stopped. (On 2207 check 15 amp. control fuse and 15 amp. exciter field fuse)

PLATE 3700-6 DIESEL ENGINE-RIGHT SIDE

SECTION 2 

COOLING SYSTEM Description: 

Water is drawn from the radiators by a centrifugal water pump located on the front end of the engine. This pump circulates water to the engine block where it surrounds the cylinder liners and circulates up through the passages in the cylinder heads and passes into the outlet manifold then back to the radiators.

Filling Cooling System: 

The cooling system contains approximately 290 gal. and may be filled through the filler pipe located along the left side of the cab skirt near front end or through the filler pipe extension on the roof. Fill the cooling system until water runs out the stand pipe. It is best to have the engine idling when system is being filled, to remove air pockets. 

Add water very slowly if engine is hot and then only when engine is idling. Do not add cold water to a hot engine that has just been drained as this will crack and warp cylinder heads and liners.

Specially treated water should be used in the cooling system to offset the accumulation of scale or foreign matter which contributes to pitting and overheating the engine. 

The engine should be operated with the water level above the minimum water level mark (2" From Bottom of Water Glass) 

and the Engine Should Not Be Operated With Water Below This 

Level. Valve on pipe directly below water glass must be open to get true water level reading (See Plate 3700-5)

Engine Cooling Water Temperature Control: 

1. Thermostatic radiator shutter control. a. The cooling water temperature is controlled. by automatic shutters and should maintain the engine temperature between 144° F. and 1600 F. 

2. Emergency water control: a If water temperature gauge indicates above 1800 F. check the top and front radiator shutters to see if they are open.

PLATE 3700-7 DIESEL ENGINE-LEFT SIDE

If either shutter is closed, break seal and move corresponding emergency shutter valve to the opposite position. 

These valves are located inside engine room behind radiator fan compartment and just inside left front door. b. Check to see that shutter has opened. c. Check water temperature gauge and if temperature remains above 180° F. there is some other cause of overheating and Engine Should Be Shut Down until condition is corrected.

If the water is to be drained, open drain valve near left front corner of engine. In addition to the drain valve is a drain plug in the bottom of the water pump. 

SECTION 3  Description: 

The lubricating oil is stored in the engine sump which classifies the engine as a wet sump type. The system is a pressure system using a positive displacement pump. The oil supply to pump is drawn through a strainer in the bottom of the sump. 

Oil may be added by removing cover on filler in crankcase (See Plate 3700-6) and pouring required amount into the engine.

With the engine idling, the oil level should always be between the "high" and "low" marks on the bayonet gauge in the right front corner of crankcase (preferably above halfway mark). 

When starting a cold engine, allow it to idle for some time and see that pressure starts to build up almost immediately. 

The pressure should rise on a cold engine to about 45 lb. and will run approximately 35 lb. at full throttle (625 r.p.m.) with engine warm. At idle, pressure should be at least 15 lb. 

With normal operation, the lubricating oil pressure should not drop below 18 lb. per square inch with engine running full throttle (625 r.p.m.)

PLATE 3700-8 FUEL TANK-LEFT SIDE

If Oil Pressure Drops Below 18 lb. With Full Throttle, Stop The Engine Until Trouble Is Corrected. 

Low Oil Pressure Shut Down: 

If the lubricating oil pressure should fail the engine will be stopped by a low oil pressure shut-down device. (See 

Part 1 end of Section 7 for explanation). 

A fuel tank is mounted under the center of the unit with a capacity of 700 gal. (See Plate 3700-8). At the bottom of the tank is a sump from which the fuel pump draws fuel oil through the suction line and suction filter. Fuel can only be drawn from the supply sump if the emergency cut-off valve is open. After leaving the pump, fuel is pumped through a filter to the fuel injection pumps. The fuel pumps use only a part of the fuel pumped to them.

Emergency Fuel Cut Off Valve (See General Section all Loco motives)

Fuel Pump: 

The fuel pump assembly consists of an electric motor and pump of the rotor gear type. The gear pump is driven by the motor shaft through a flexible coupling. Fuel pressure should be 10-25 lb. at Header to fuel injection pumps and 25-35 lb. at Filter. If fuel pressure drops below 5 lb. engine will stop. 

Do not leave the fuel pump running unnecessarily.

The Electrical cabinet in Front of Cab includes the Following} 

1. Reverser (REV) 

Changes direction of flow of current through traction motor fields to change direction of locomotive movement.
