When operating in multiple unites, a wheel slip on a trailing unit will also cause the wheel slip light to light. 

Since wheels do not slip continuously, the wheel slip The connections from the light will not burn continuously. wheel slip relay to the power plants are such that the power For this is automatically reduced when the wheels slip. 

Unless rail reason wheel slips are usually intermittent. conditions are extremely bad, it will not be necessary to apply sand.

When applying sand be sure that wheels are not slipping at the time sand is being applied. Due to the fact that sand is injurious to traction motors and electrical equipment, use sand only when necessary.

Air Braking With Power: 

When it is necessary to apply air brakes with full power supplied by the locomotive, the throttle must be reduced to the sixth notch. It is imperative that the locomotive brakes be released and held in the release position when the locomotive is supplying power. 

When preparing to stop with power applied to the locomotive and brakes applied to the train, it is necessary to reduce the throttle as the speed of the train decreases. The

throttle must be in idle before the train comes to dead stop.

These instructions are not to interfere with operating instructions covering the use of air brakes. 

Throttle Emergency Stop Button: 

This button is located on the end of the throttle lever. 

Its purpose is to stop all engines of the locomotive in case of an emergency. It is operated by pushing the button and moving the throttle lever past idle position.

An engine can also be stopped by pushing in the red "Emergency Stop" button on the engine above the governor. After using this button, the overspeed stop located below the "Emergency Stop" button must be reset before engine can be started. 

Operating as Helper Locomotive: 

When operating as a helper locomotive, especially when helping a steam engine, care must be taken not to allow the helper engine to assume more than its share of the load. 

Knowing the length of the grade to be traversed, the time on the grade can be closely estimated. This time will set the minimum speed and maximum loading allowable. If the estimated time on the grade is above 2 hours, the load indicator should not be allowed to go higher than 1020 amps. If the load indicator pointer does indicate excessive loading, reduce the throttle until the pointer comes within the continuous rating of 1020 amps. 

Towing Locomotive: 

1. When necessary to prepare the locomotive for deadheading, stop the engine. 

2. Set up air brake equipment as follows: 

a. Place handle of both brake valves in RUNNING. 

b. Remove both brake valve handles. c. Move brake valve cutout cock to DEAD position. d. Open dead engine cock on distributing valve. e. Reduce the adjustment on the safety valve on the distributing valve so that the brake cylinder pressure will not exceed 25 pounds. f. See that radiator shutters are closed.

Isolating an Engine While Under Power (Multiple Unit Oper ation): 

If it becomes necessary to take an engine "off the line" while the locomotive is operating under power it should be 

1.

Pull manual lay shaft lever shut and hold until engine stops. 

2.

When bell starts ringing place isolation switch in start position. 

3. Turn fuel pump switch off.

Starting and Placing Engine on Line While Under Power (Multiple Unit Operation) 

1. Start engine in usual manner. 

2. After lube oil pressure builds up and engine temperature rises to at least 1250 F., place isolation switch in Run position. 

Precautions During Locomotive Operation: 

With the engine running, After 

1. Check the battery ammeter. this meter should always indicate a charge. the locomotive has been in operation for a period of time, the reading should show a small charge only. A continuously high charge reading should be reported for attention. 

2. See that the control air pressure gauge indicates 80 pounds pressure. 

3. Normal lubricating oil pressure should be not less than 

6 pounds with engine idling and 24 pounds at full throttle. If oil pressure drops below 24 pounds at full throttle, stop engine and do not operate until cause is corrected. 

4. Fuel oil pressure on the gauge in the cab should not be less than 5 pounds. 

5. The lube oil level should be between the "low and high" level marks on the bayonet gauge. 

6. Engine cooling water temperature should be maintained at 1650 F. plus or minus 150. Do not exceed 1950 F.

The cooling water radiator shutters and cooling fan are automatically operated. Manual controls are provided to permit adjustment in case of failure of any of the temperature control equipment.

1. Snap OFF Locomotive Run switch, Control breaker, Fuel Pump breaker, and Fuel Pump Switch. 

2. Stop engine. 

3. Lock throttle in IDLE or NEUTRAL. 

4. Remove reverse handle (if used). 

5. Pull main battery switch. 

6. Apply hand brake. 

7. Snap OFF cab light switch. 

8. Water system must be protected from freezing in cold weather either by adding heat to the cooling system by connecting to the standby steam supply, or by completely draining the system. 

Changing Ends (In Multiple Unit Operation): 

Before Leaving Cab: 

1. Move selector to OFF and throttle to IDLE. REMOVE REVERSE HANDLE.

2. Make 20 lb. brake pipe reduction and lap brake valve.

Close brake valve cutout cock, or move to TRLG. position. 

3. Remove both brake valve handles. 

4. Snap OFF Locomotive Run switch, Control breaker, and Fuel Pump breaker. LEAVE ON FUEL PUMP SWITCH. 

Engines will idle three or four minutes with fuel pumps stopped which will allow time to re-energize fuel pump control from Fuel Pump Breaker in new leading cab. 

New Leading Cab: 

1. Snap ON Fuel Pump and Control breakers. 

2. Insert brake handles. 

3. Open brake valve cutout cock and make air tests. 

4. Replace reverse handle and move selector to desired position. 

5. Snap ON Locomotive Run switch when ready to move locomotive. 

TROUBLE SHOOTING No Fuel Pressure When Fuel Pump Switch Is on: 

1. Fuel Pump fails to run, or stops; a. Main battery switch not closed. b. Fuel pump breaker tripped on lead unit. c. Fuel pump switch not closed. d. Control jumper between units not making contact. 

2. Fuel pump runs but little or no pressure shows: a. No fuel in tank. b. Emergency fuel cutoff tripped. c. Battery weak. d. Leaks in pump suction line. e. Worn pump packing rings. f. Pump relief valve sticking. g. Clogged pressure filter. 

Engine Fails To Rotate When Start Button Is Pressed: 

1. Fuel pump breaker not closed in leading unit. 

2. Weak battery. If engine room lights go very dim or out generator is receiving starting current but battery is too weak to rotate generator. 

3. Starting Contactors not closing. a. Check isolation switch (must be "Start). b. Out interlock on EF (must be closed).

Engine Rotates but Fails to Fire: 

1. Overspeed tripped. 

2. Low oil shutdown button on governor not reset. 

3. No fuel pressure. 

4. Isolation switch in "Stop" position.

If Locomotive Does Not Move When Throttle Is Opened: 

1. Isolation switch not in "Run" position. 2. TV contactor not closing a. Ground Relay (Tripped) b. Starting contactors (Stuck closed). 

If Engine Does Not Load Up: 

1. Diesel engine isolated. 

2. Control breaker or locomotive run switch not closed. 

3. Control air pressure less than 70 lbs. (Should be 

80 lbs. on gauge in cab.) 

4. 25 amp. control fuse blown. 

5. 25 amp. excitation fuse blown. 6. AF contactor not closing. Oil pressure switch should be closed in line to AF coil. 

7. TV contactor not closed. Check. a. Isolation switch position. b. Ground relay. Must NOT be tripped. 

c. Starting contactors. Must be OPEN. 

8. Power contactors not closed. Check a. AF interlock. Must be closed. b. TDR relay. Must be closed. 

9. TDR relay not energized. Check a. Blower relay, BL. Must be de-energized. b. TV contactor. Must be closed. 

10. EF contactor not energized. Check a. Power contactor interlocks. Must be CLOSED. b. Wheel slip relays. Normally closed contacts must be making.

If Battery Charging Ammeter Shows Zero: 

1. Battery charging contactor must be closed. 

2. 100 amp. battery charging fuse must be good. 

3. Auxiliary generator cut-out switch must be closed. 

If Locomotive Suddenly Becomes Inoperative: 

1. Check control circuit breaker. 

2. Check 25 amp. control fuse. 

3. Check ground relay. 

4. Check 25 amp. exciter fuse.

If Engine Fails to Stop: 

Stop engine with manual layshaft control.

Failure to stop by use of "Stop" button may be due to the control circuit breaker being tripped or a faulty governor.

SECTION 2 COOLING SYSTEM. 

Description: 

The water leaves the engine from the water header and is piped to the radiators, After being cooled by the air circulated thru the radiators by the radiator cooling fan,

the water returns to the pump saction. A constant head is maintained on the system by the equalizer line to the expansion tank. a. Thermometer and Indicator Lights

Connections for the remote reading thermotmeter and the high temperature indicator light on the control panel are provided in the engine to radiator line. The high temperature switch is set to light the indicator light at 190° F. 

b. Temperature Control and Servo-motor 

The thermocouple bulb operating the temperature regulating servo-motor of the jacket water system is located in the engine water discharge line. This servo-motor controls the two radiators, side shutters, the top shutters over the cooling fan, and the 3-speed fan.

The temperature control switches operated by the servomotor are set to open the top shutter and to control the 3 speeds of the fan as required. The servo-motor directly controls the shutter operation and the fan speed position. 

This device is so calibrated as to maintain an average temperature of approximately 1600 F. 

To Fill Cooling System: 

Water should be added to the system when the level in the expansion tank sight gauge drops below the "Low Level" mark behind the gauge. Water is added at the hose connection near the front truck at the right side of the locomotive. 

Add until it runs out the common drain pipe. Do not operate engine when water is below "Low Level" mark.

To Drain Cooling System: 

To drain the radiator and jacket water cooling systems, open valves V3 and V8 in the drain line and remove plug in bottom of each of the water pumps. 

SECTION 3  Description: 

The engine lubricating oil system is of the pressure type designed to supply a continuous flow of oil to all bearing surfaces as well as to act as a cooling agent for the pistons. After engine lubrication and piston cooling, the oil flows to the engine subbase. 

Lubricating Oil Gauge Dip Stick: 

Each side of the oil level gauge dip stick is calibrated.

One side is marked "ENGINE STOPPED," the other "ENGINE RUNNING".

The amount of oil circulating in the system is represented by the space on the dip stick between the "OIL CHANGE-NEW FILL" 

and "FULL ENGINE" marks. (See Plate 4700-4)

Adding Oil: 

Lubricating oil need not be added until the oil level has reached the "Add Oil" mark. Oil should then be added until the level is at the "Full Engine" or "Full System" mark on the dip stick, depending on whether the engine is stopped or running. 

Oil Pressure: 

A connection is made from a point in the engine lubricating system to a diaphragm in the shut-down mechanism of the governor. 

Pressures of less than 4 psi. at idle will, after a certain time delay, cause the shut-down mechanism to trip and shut down the engine. Pressures of less than 15 psi. at full engine speed will actuate the shut-down mechanism without a time delay.

A low lubricating oil pressure switch within the governor connects to an amber indicator light on the control panel. 

Lighting of this light indicates a failure of the engine lubricating oil pressures.

The motor driven supply pump draws fuel from the oil tank and delivers it thru the fuel oil filter to the engine inlet. The by-pass relief valve protects the system in case of clogging of the filter. The gauge on the control panel indicates the pressure at the fuel supply header. 

The fuel supply headers on each side of the engine are connected to each injection pump. More fuel is pumped thru the injection system than is needed by the pumps, and a pressure is maintained by the relief valve at the header outlet. 

Fuel Tank: 

The fuel tank has 3 sight glasses for indicating the level of the fuel. All filler pipes are provided with 

Protectoseal caps. The units are provided with two fill pipes, one on either side of the locomotive. 

Emergency Fuel Cut Off Valve (See General section all loco motives).

SECTION 5 ELECTRICAL SYSTEM Traction Motor Cutout Knife Switches: 

These are located on the cab side in the high voltage cabinet. 

Switch TMCO 1 cuts out traction motors on No. 1 truck 

Switch TMCO 2 cuts out traction motors on No. 2 truck.

Main generator power is automatically reduced 50% when a truck is cut out. This is done by energizing the engine governor overriding solenoid to send the load regulator to minimum field.

WHEN OPERATING WITH A PAIR OF TRACTION MOTORS CUT OUT, LOAD CURRENT MUST BE RESTRICTED TO ONE-HALF OF MAXIMUM CON- 

TINUOUS. This means the load current must not exceed onehalf of the highest reading on the white scale. 

Low Power Control: 

1. To aid in spotting cars, making soft couplings and in humping operations, locomotives are equipped with "Low Power" control. Pressing the, "Low Power" button at the control station will reduce power from that normally available as the throttle is notched out. The load regulator remains in minimum field in all throttle notches instead of moving clockwise from minimum field in the first and succeeding notches. 

2. To return unit to normal power, the "High Power" button must be pressed. This allows the load regulator to travel from minimum field to its normal position. 

3. "High Power" operation will cut in automatically with the throttle beyond 4th notch or 540 RPM. High power operation nullifies low power operation which will have to be reset by pressing the "Low Power" button if low power is again desired. CAUTION: Do not press "Low Power" button beyond automatic high-power cut-in point, to avoid power cycling.

Electrical Cabinet: 

The electrical control cabinet, located in the rear wall of the cab, houses the engine control panel, distribution panel, and high voltage equipment. (See Plate 4700-5)

Gu AF: 

Energizes auxiliary generator field, exciter field, fan generator field, and fan-shutter controls.

SECTION 6 AIR SYSTEM Air Compressor: 

Each locomotive is equipped with a Westinghouse two stage air cooled air compressor to supply air for the air brake and control air systems.

Draining Main Air Reservoirs: 

Each locomotive is equipped with two main air reservoirs located underneath the carbody between the trucks. 

In order to prevent moisture from being carried over into the air brake and control air systems, these reservoirs should be drained at each crew change.

Main reservoir air pressure is maintained between 130 to 140 pounds per square inch and the control air pressure should be 80 pounds per square inch, plus or minus 3 pounds.

Operating Instructions Operating Instructions】 Operating Instructions Operating Instructions Vapor Water Heaters. 

Steam Generating Tenders. 

Page 7100 7200 7300 7400 7500 7600

GENERAL BASIC DESCRIPTION GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

The Vapor Clarkson steam generators are designed and manufactured by the Vapor Car Heating Company and are used for heating and cooling of Diesel powered passenger trains. 

The Vapor Clarkson steam generator has a heat release of (approximately) 1,000,000 B. T.U. per cubic foot of combustion space. 

This heat output is transmitted to water to produce the necessary steam for adequate train heating. This steam generator is a combination of coiled water tubing, forced circulation of water and steam, pressurized fuel spray and electric ignition. 

GENERAL DESCRIPTION OF CONSTRUCTION 

The Vapor Clarkson steam generator is comprised basically of three sets of coils completely encircling the combustion chamber, nested and connected in series, and designed to accept a maximum heat transfer from an intense oil fire. The coils are staggered to split the flow of hot combustion gases into thin films that wipe over every part of the coils before emerging into the stack, where an additional coil has been placed in the path of the stack gases which further reduces the temperature of these gases, and transfers the heat regained into the incoming feed water. 

GENERAL DESCRIPTION OF OPERATION 

Combustion air, under forced draft, mixes with the pressure atomized Diesel fuel oil spray in the fire pot and is ignited by a continuous electric spark; the hot gases of combustion flow in the opposite direction to the course of the feed water, through the coils. 

An electric motor operates the fuel pump, water pump, blower and ignition system. Water is pumped into the continuous coil and converted into steam as it progresses first through the economizer coil, the outer set of coils, then the intermediate coils and finally through the inner coils. 

The supply of fuel is regulated to evaporate 90% to 95% of the water pumped through the coils. The excess water flushes scale and sludge from the coils and is carried over with the steam into the steam separator where the water and sludge is removed before the steam flows into the train line. 

From the inner coil the steam enters the steam separator at an angle that will impart a centrifugal action which separates the carry-over water from the steam. The carry-over water settles to the bottom of the separator and is returned through the steam trap to the heat exchanger, where it gives heat to the incoming feed water before returning to the water supply tank.

USE OF STEAM GENERATOR REMOTE CONTROLS ON LOCOMOTIVES WHEN OPERATING IN PASSENGER SERVICE 

The steam generator remote controls located in the cab of the locomotive consist of the following: (See Plate 7100-1) 

1. A two-way toggle switch marked Master Switch with On and Off positions. 

2. Two push button switches marked Train Line Shut Off and Separator Blow-down. 

3. A steam gauge to indicate steam pressure in the train line. 

4. A green indicator light marked Boiler Off will light in case the steam generator shuts down for any reason. 

5. A red indicator light marked Hot Engine will light in case of excessive Diesel engine cooling water temperatures on one or more main engines. When indicator light indicates hot engine, it will be necessary to stop the train and determine the cause. 

6. A yellow indicator light marked Low Oil which will light when the oil pressure becomes low on one or more of the main engines. When indicator light indicates low oil, it will be necessary to stop the train and determine the cause. 

7. Violet light to indicate automatic separator blowdown operation.

The steam generator remote control equipment located in the engine room consists of the following: 

1. Separator blow-down magnet valve which allows main reservoir air to flow to piston on separator blowdown valve when separator blow-down push button in cab is pressed or automatic blow-down operates. 

2. Separator blow-down piston which opens separator blow-down valve. 

The steam generator remote controls operate as follows: (See Plate 7100-1) 

1. Move Master Switch to On position: 

a. To blow down separator push in Separator Blowdown switch and hold in for 10 to 15 seconds. 

The separator should be blown down each 30 minutes. b. When locomotive is equipped with automatic separator blow-down, separator will blow-down automatically. On locomotives so equipped Master 

Switch must be OFF while in stations to prevent automatic separator blow-down. 

c. To shut off steam to trainline lift cover on 

Trainline Shut Off switch and push in on the button for 5 seconds. d. To admit steam into trainline-reset the solenoid valve to Open position by pushing down on lever located near manually operated steam shut-off valve ON STEAM GENERATOR.

MCRAY AQUAMAT 

Some locomotives are equipped with a McRay Aquamat, which is a self contained unit, located in the engine room for the treatment of the steam generator feed water. The purpose of the Aquamat is to preheat, degas and inhibit dissolved solids in the feed water. This permits the removal of the accumulated precipitate by the application of adequate blowdown facilities which keeps the coils clean and automatically maintains the temperature of the feed water at 1900 F. 

WINTER OPERATION OF STEAM GENERATORS. 

During winter operation only the No. 10 valve shall be open to supply steam for the water pump heater at all times. 

The opening of the No. 10 valve will be the only change from normal operation of the various valves. 

In the event a steam generator is shut down enroute, the following should be done: 

1. Open the No.6 valve. 

2. Close water inlet valve No.21 to the strainer tank. 

3. Open the water strainer tank drain valve No.22. 

4. Close the coil blow down valve No.2 

5. Close the separator blow down valve No.12.

This will permit steam to circulate through the coils and piping to prevent freezing. 

HEATER LINES 

On Alco Passenger Locomotives, the heater valves to the boiler water tanks and equalizing lines will be closed and used only in case of emergency.

On EMO Passenger Locomotives, the heater lines to the boiler water tanks on cab units only should be operated with the heater valve wide open. Heater valves to all equalizing lines and boiler water tanks in booster units will be closed and and used only in case of emergency.

Santa Fe CFK 4225 

STEAM GENERATOR 

PART III-SECTION 2

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

The CFK 4225 steam generator, designed and manufactured by the Vapor Car Heating Company, has a rated capacity of 2250 lb. of water evaporation per hour, and is used for heating and cooling Diesel powered trains. Operation of the steam generator is completely automatic once it has been started. 

The steam generator is comprised basically of three sets of coils, connected in series to form a single tube several hundred feet long, and completely encircling the combustion chamber. The coils are staggered to split the flow of hot combustion gases into thin films that wipe over every part of the coils before emerging into the stack, where an economizer coil is nested to further reduce the temperature of these gases and transfer the heat regained into the incoming feed water. 

Water is pumped into the coil inlet and converted into steam as it progresses through the coils. Heat is furnished by the combustion of Diesel fuel oil, which is sprayed by compressed air through the atomizing nozzle in the fuel spray head into the fire pot above the coils. Here the fine oil spray mixes with air supplied by the blower fan and is ignited by a continuous electric spark. The fire and hot gases flow first downward then outward through the coils in opposite direction to the course of the feed water through the coils. 

The supply of fuel is regulated to evaporate 90% to 95% of the water pumped through the coils. The excess water flushes scale and sludge from the coils and is carried over with the steam into the steam separator, where the water and sludge is removed before the steam flows into the trainline. 

The water collects in the bottom of the steam separator. 

When it reaches the level of the return water outlet the water flows out through the steam trap and through the heat exchanger where it gives up its heat to the incoming feed water. 

From the heat exchanger the return water flows through the return water flow indicator back to the supply tank. 

The motor drives the water pump, fuel pump and blower fan. The steam pressure switch automatically controls the speed of the motor by inserting resistors into the motor circuit. The speed of the motor regulates the amount of water fed to the coils to maintain the desired steam pressure according to the demand for steam and the setting of the steam pressure switch. 

Before entering the coils, the water passes through the fuel control valve, which admits fuel to the spray nozzle in direct proportion to the amount of water entering the coils. 

The fuel after passing through the fuel control valve also passes through the steam temperature limit control, which regulates the amount of fuel to the nozzle according to the steam temperature. The speed of the motor controls the speed of the blower to furnish the proper amount of air for efficient combustion of the fuel.

DIRECTIONS FOR OPERATING 

(For identification of parts referred to see views on) (Plates 7200-2 and 7200-3)

BEFORE STARTING 

1. See that the following valves are OPEN. a. Water suction stop valve. b. Coil shut-off valve. c. Separator return water valve. d. Pressure regulator switch steam admission valve. e. Atomizing air valve. f. Steam pressure gauge steam admission valve. 

2. See that following valves are CLOSED. a. Drain valve in water suction line. b. Coil blow-down valve. c. Water pump test valve. d. Washout solution inlet and outlet valves. e. Manual trainline stop valve. f. Manual fuel by-pass valve. 

3. See that water strainer tank cover is on tightly.

4. See that reset button on control panel is IN. 5. See that stack switch reset button is IN. 

6. See that high temperature switch reset button is IN. 

7. See that steam pressure switch is set to desired pressure. 

STARTING STEAM GENERATOR 

1. Close main switch. 2. Close ignition motor toggle switch. 

3. Fill steam generator coils as follows: 

a. Open separator blow-down valve, to drain steam separator, and latch valve in full open position. b. When water disappears at try cock on separator, close the separator blow-down valve. c. Turn operating switch to "TEST" position. 

This will run the water pump. d. When water runs a steady stream at try cock 

OPEN SEPARATOR BLOW-DOWN VALVE. 

It may take 

5 to 10 minutes to fill the steam generator with water, if empty. e. Turn operating switch to OFF. f. During the time the steam generator is being filled with water, check to see that spark is available, by looking through peep hole glass at top of steam generator. 

4. Open atomizing air admission valve and check the air pressure on gauge, should be 45 lb. 

5. Turn operating switch to START. immediately. 

Fire should light 

6. After fire has operated for 2 or 3 minutes and 50 lbs.

Routine Inspection to be Made While Steam Generator is Operat ing: 

1. Check return water flow indicator, valve should cycle 

6 to 12 times per minute. If valve cycles more than 

12 times per minute this indicates insufficient fuel is being burned. If valve cycles less than 6 times per minute this indicates too much fuel is being burned. Report either condition at terminal. 

2. Check the water pump operation on water pressure gage.

The pressure should not exceed 450 lb. The gage If should be steady; as jumping action indicates faulty pump valves or air leak in water suction line. feed water is too hot, water pump may become vapor bound and give jumping action to gage. In this event close water tank heater line valve. 

3. Check water supply in water tanks. 

For Short Stops: 

TO STOP STEAM GENERATOR 

1. Close trainline stop valve. (The fire will cycle "on" and "off" to maintain pressure on steam generator).

For Terminal Stops (End of Trip): 

1. Close trainline stop valve. 

2. Set up the steam pressure switch to 200 lb. pressure. 

3. At 200 lb. pressure turn operating switch to OFF. 

4. Open coil blow-down valve. (Knob on coil blow-down

TROUBLE SHOOTING 

1. Cause of shut down on steam generating units. a. Pressure setting of pressure control switch satisfied. b. Safety devices, which ring alarm and light green light: 

1. Stack switch. Set to open when stack temperature reaches 9000 F. 

2. High temperature switch. Shuts down the steam genarator should the temperature of the steam become excessive (approximately 6200 F.). This switch is actuated by the melting of a metal slug and it is necessary to allow approximately 5 minutes time for this metal to solidify before the switch can be reset after it has tripped due to super-heating of the steam. 

3. Out-fire relay operation: If fire fails to light when starting generator, motor will stop after 45 seconds. If fire goes out while generator is operating, motor will stop when stack temperature drops to 200°; this will take approximately 2 minutes. 

Check cause I lack of water, lack of fuel, no atomizing air. 

c. Coil blow-down switch not contacting. No indication by alarm or lights. d. Electrical overload switch: Shuts off lights in steam generator gage panel, no alarm light or bell. 

2. If motor will not run on turning operating switch to TEST 

or START position: 

a. Check to see if main switch is closed. b. Check coil blow-down valve. This valve must be closed and electrical contact on coil blow-down switch made. c. Check motor overload switch on relay panel, must be in. d. Check stack switch reset button, must be in. e. Check fuses in steam generator circuit. 

3. If fire fails to start and motor runs. a. Check the manual fuel by-pass valve, this valve must be CLOSED. b. Check the fuel key metering valve, should be open ONLY one quarter to one half turn. c. Check fuel and water supply. d. Check for leaks in water and fuel suction lines. e. Check to see if ignition motor is running and if there is a spark at the spark plugs.

When steam generator is shut down from operation of a safety device, the steam generator coils must be refilled with water, as outlined in starting instructions, before operation is resumed. 

Directions for Draining Steam Generator and Accessories of Water During Layover in Freezing Weather: 

1. Follow steam generator terminal shut-down procedure. 

2. Leave coil blow-down valve open. 

3. Blow-down steam separator and latch valve open. 

4. Drain water supply tanks. 

5. Close coil shut-off valve. Turn operating switch to test and allow water from pipes to be pumped out coil blow-down. 

6. Open strainer tank drain valve. 

7. Break all copper tubing joints and blow out tubes with compressed air. Remove drain plugs from water pump and blow out.

Santa Fe DRK-4530 and OK-4630 

STEAM GENERATORS 

По 

PART III-SECTION 3

GENERAL DESCRIPTION The DRK-4530 and OK-4630 Vapor Clarkson steam genera tors are very similar in all respects. They both have a rated capacity of 3,000 lb. water evaporation per hour. Operation of these units is automatic with exception of starting. 

The steam generating part of the unit consists of sets of coiled water tubing, nested and connected in series with each other. In addition a fourth coil has been added in the exhaust chamber which acts as an economizer and preheats water as it enters the boiler tubing. The heat is derived from the combustion of Diesel fuel oil, which is sprayed into the boiler under pressure and fired by electrical spark, with a forced draft, causing the burning gases to flow downward through the coils and up into the exhaust stack. 

The supply of fuel is regulated to evaporate 90% to 95% of the water pumped through the coils. The excess water tends to flush the sludge and scale from the tubes, over into the steam separator. When the water level in the separator reaches the return outlet, the water flows through the heat exchanger, where it heats incoming water, to the supply tank. 

The motor converter drives the blower, water pump and fuel pump at a constant speed. The water by-pass regulator automatically controls the steam generator output by regulating the amount of water fed to the coils. Before entering the coils, the water passes through the servo-fuel control which admits fuel to the spray nozzle in direct proportion to the amount of water entering the coils. The servo-control also adjusts the damper to admit proper amount of air for the efficient combustion of fuel.

Trainline steam pressure is regulated by adjusting the hand wheel on the water by-pass regulator.

a. Atomizing air shut-off valve b. Coil shut-off valve

a. Coil blown-down valve No.2. b. Layover connection shut-off valve

TO FILL 

1. Open atomizing air shut-off valve No.1, fill test valve 

No.4 and latch separator blow-down valve.

Close separator blow-down valve when separator is completely drained. 

2. Close main switch and turn control switch to "Fill" position. 

3. While coils are filling check to see that spark is available. Recheck all valves. 

4. When water discharges from the fill-test valve, close fill-test valve, and turn control switch to the "OFF" position. If coils are empty it will take about 5 minutes to fill the steam generator coils. 

TO START 

Do not start steam generator unless you are CERTAIN 

COILS ARE FILLED. 

1. Latch open the separator blow-down valve and turn control switch to "Run" position. For easy starting be sure control switch is in "OFF" position long enough for the motor to come to a complete stop. 

2. Close separator blow-down valve when steam pressure gage reaches 50 p.s.i. 

3. Open steam separator valve several times during first few minutes of operation to eliminate excess water in separator. 

4. Set water by-pass regulator line pressure. 

No.111 to desired train 

5. After the train line is coupled, open remote control valve No.7 by depressing the latch, then open the manual trainline stop valve No.15. 

NOTE: Check return water flow after steam output has settled down. On 3,000 lb. units the return water flow indicator should cycle from 4 to 12 times per minute. 

Steam generator should come up to pressure in one or two minutes, even though it may take 10 or 15 minutes to build up the required operating steam pressure in the trainline.

RUNNING ATTENTION 

1. Open separator blow-down valve 

No.12 for 10 to 15 seconds once every 30 minutes. This can be done by pressing blow-down button on cab control panel. The 

Master Switch must be ON. This is NOT NECESSARY on locomotives equipped with automatic blow-down. 

2. Turn the handle on fuel filter when locomotive is stopped.

TO SHUT DOWN STEAM GENERATOR 

For short stops it is only necessary to close the "stop and check" valve. The fire will cycle and maintain operating pressure in the steam generator.

NOTE: On locomotives equipped with automatic separator blow down the "Master Switch" should be turned "OFF" when locomotive is standing in the station to forestall the automatic blow-down feature. Even though the trainline is shut off, the automatic separator blow-down will operate unless the "Master Switch" is "OFF".

For terminal stops proceed as follows: 

1. Close "stop and check" valve, remote control trainline shut-off valve and manual trainline shut-off valve. 

2. Set water by-pass valve to maximum. When the steam generator pressure gage reaches 200 p.s.i., turn control switch to the "OFF" position. 

3. Open coil blow-down valve. When pressure drops to 100 p.s.i. close the valve and complete blow-down operation through the separator blow-down. Close the separator blow-down valve. 

4. Fill steam generator coil with water. 

5. Close atomizing air valve and open main switch. 

6. During winter operation the No.6 valve on the steam generator (tagged with metal tag) should remain open at all times on engines. 

TROUBLE SHOOTING 

If one of the protective switches (magnetic overload relay, thermal overload switch, coil blow-down valve switch, stack switch) operates to shut down the steam generator, the alarm will ring and the "boiller off" signal will flash on the remote control panel.

Turn control switch "OFF" and use following instructions as a guide in locating the trouble. 

Motor and Burner Shut Down During Operation: 

1. Blown Fuses The alarm will not ring and instrument lights will go out. Check main boiler fuse (or circuit breaker) in locomotive low voltage cabinet. Check control fuses in steam generator control cabinet.

Ζητά 

TRAINLINE PRESSURE ADJUSTING HANDLE BY-PASS REGULATOR SHUTOFF VALVE MANUAL WATER DY PASS VALVE PLATE 7300-5 FEED WATER CONTROLS OK-4630

2. Overload Reset Button "OUT". The alarm will ring and the instrument lights will remain ON. Turn control switch off, check for a hot blower, water pump bearings and poorly adjusted pulley belts. Check setting of belt tension adjuster. 

3. Stack Switch Reset Button "OUT". The alarm will ring and instrument lights will remain ON. The high temperature contacts in the stack switch are open. Turn the control switch off. Open separator blow-down valve and drain separator. Close the separator blow-down valve, push in stack switch reset button, refill coils with water and start steam generator.

Motor Starts But Burner Does Not: 

If the fire fails to light, outfire contacts on stack switch will not close and after 45 seconds outfire relay will open circuit and shut down the steam generator. The alarm will ring. Turn control switch off and check following list for possible cause of failure: 

1. Ignition Failure 

no spark is visible through peephole glass. Check ignition circuit fuses in steam generator control cabinet. Check for loose connections and for broken or grounded wires that might ground or interrupt circuit. 

2. Low Atomizing Air Pressure 

Low air pressure will fail to lift diaphragm in fuel spray head. The needle valve to nozzle remains closed and prevents admission_of_fuel to fire pot. Atomizing air pressure should be 70-75 p.s.i. Be sure the air admission valve is fully open. 

Check strainer screens at air pressure reducing valve and in air lines. 

3. Low Fuel Manifold Pressure 

fuel filter several times. cause low manifold pressure. should be 150 p.s.i. 

4. Low Fuel Nozzle Pressure 

Turn handle on suction line

A slight suction leak may Fuel manifold pressure 

Lack of water causes fuel servo to limit the supply of fuel entering the nozzle. 

If water supply is almost stopped, the servo may come down far enough to actuate cut-out switch and close solenoid valve to shut off atomizing air.

Be sure that pump belts have proper tension, the water pump test valve is closed, cover on water strainer tank is tight, and that drain valves are tightly closed. 

Open and close water by-pass regulator several times to to free regulator from possible sediment. Water pressure should register between 225-350 p.s.i. and not over 450 p.s.i. 

Irregular Trainline Pressure: 

1. Burner Cycles On and Off. Insufficient water delivery causes steam generator to run in superheat and steam temperature limit switch operates to protect coils from overheating.

Lower 

2. Safety Valves Blow. Shut down steam generator. trainline pressure by adjusting handle of water by-pass regulator and start steam generator again.

ITEMS TO REPORT 

1. Water pressure greater than 450 p.s.1. at any time. 

2. Excessive stack temperature. 

3. Fluctuation of fuel manifold pressure. 

4. Frequent cycling of burner. 

5. Water flow indicator not cycling. 

6. Water by-pass regulator inoperative. 

7. Faulty operation of steam generator for any reason.

Santa Fe 

OK-4740 STEAM GENERATORS 

PART III-SECTION 4

The OK-4740 Vapor Clarkson steam generators are rated at 4,500 lb. water evaporation per hour. The OK-4740 is a new design and a definite departure from previous steam generators. It is larger in capacity and size which made it necessary to install the water pump and blower motor in the vertical position. The blower position has been changed so that it now rotates about a vertical axis. The water pump is driven by the vertically mounted motor through reduction gearing. For location of equipment on the steam generator see Plate 7400-2.

The principal of operation is the same as other Vapor Clarkson steam generators. The boiler coils are rearranged and include both water wall and economizer coils. The ignition voltage is increased to 12,000 volts. 

Plate 7400-3 shows a complete view of the OK-4740 steam generator in a schematic arrangement. 

The trainline pressure is adjusted by setting water bypass regulator. This is a new regulator that is adjustable to only a few positions and is held in position by a pin that penetrates the plate on the water by-pass regulator. The re- gulating lever is spring loaded to the "OFF" position, therefore, holding pin must be securely fastened.

On OK model steam generators the valves designated by ODD numbers must be OPEN during operation, while those designated by EVEN numbers must be CLOSED during operation. 

Normally open valves are fitted with CROSS type handles, while normally closed valves are fitted with the standard 

ROUND handles.

TO FILL STEAM GENERATOR 

1. Open the atomizing air shut-off valve No. 1 and fill test valve No. 4. Latch open the separator blow-down valve No. 12 to drain the steam separator. Close the separator blow-down valve when the separator is completely drained. 

2. Put main switch ON and turn the control switch No. 102 to FILL. 

3. While coils are filling see that spark is available for ignition. Check all valves. 

4. When water discharges from fill-test valve, turn control switch OFF and close fill test valve 

TO START 

1. Latch open separator blow-down valve, and turn control switch to RUN. (Do not start boiler without being sure coils are full of water). 

2. Close separator blow-down valve when steam pressure reaches 100 p.s.i. 

3. Open separator blow-down valve several times during first few minutes of operation. 

4. Set water by-pass regulator to desired pressure. 

5. After trainline is coupled, open the remote control trainline shut-off valve No. 7 by depressing reset lever 

No. 7a. Then open stop and check valve.

NOTES: 

a. Check return water flow after steam generator has settled down to a steady output. Should cycle 4 to 12 times per minute. b. Steam generator should reach full operating pressure in about 5 minutes. Trainline pressure may require longer to build up to desired operating pressure. 

RUNNING ATTENTION 

1. Blow down the steam separator at least once every 30 minutes. This is done automatically on units equipped with Automatic Separator Blow-down. While in intermediate terminals, turn Master Switch OFF to nullify Automatic Blow-down feature. 

2. Turn the handle on fuel filter (No. 206) during stops and drain air filter.

TO SHUT DOWN STEAM GENERATOR 

1. For short stops it is only necessary to close the remote control valve. The fire will cycle and maintain operating pressure on coils. 

2. Close stop and check valve No. 15 and remote control trainline shut-off valve. 

3. Set water by-pass regulator No. 111 to maximum.

When steam pressure reaches maximum turn the control switch OFF. 

4. Open coil blow-down valve. When steam generator pressure drops to 100 p.s.i. close valve. 

5. Open separator blow-down valve and complete blow-down process. When process is completed, close blow-down valve and fill coils with water. 

6. Close atomizing air shut-off valve No. 1, and turn the main switch OFF.

NOTE: 

a. When starting, never fail to make sure coils are full of water. The fact that separator has water in it means nothing. Drain separator then run motor until water discharges from open "fill test" valve. b. On locomotives equipped with automatic separator blowdown, the "Master Switch" should be turned OFF when locomotive is left standing in station to forestall automatic blow-down operation. Even though trainline is shut off, the automatic blow-down will operate unless the "Master

Switch" is "OFF". c. During winter operation, the No.6 valve on the steam generator (tagged with metal tag) should remain open at all times.

TROUBLE SHOOTING 

When the boiler is shut down due to the action of some protective device, turn the control switch "OFF" and use the following instructions as a guide in locating trouble: 

Motor Shuts Down or Fails to Start: 

1. Open circuit breakers: The alarm rings and instrument lights go out if control circuit breakers are open. If the main fuse or circuit breaker is out, the alarm will not ring. 

2. Overload reset button No. 106 "OUT". Alarm rings and instrument lights remain on. Open main switch to shut off alarm, then check for hot motor or water pump bearing, overheated fuel pump or gear drive assembly. If all appear OK, close main switch, reset overload button and start as usual. 

3. Stack switch reset button "OUT". Alarm rings and instrument lights remain on. Open the separator blow-down valve No. 12 and turn control switch to FILL. When water begins to flow from this valve, push in stack switch reset button and restart the steam generator.

OVERLOAD RESET BUTTON CIRCUIT BREAKERS STEAM TEMPERATURE LIMIT CONTROL RESET BUTTON PLATE 7400-4 CONTROL CABINET FUEL FILTER

4. Coil blow-down valve partially open. Alarm rings and instrument lights remain on. Turn control switch off, be sure coil blow-down valve is properly closed and locking pin seated in closed position, and then restart in usual manner. Alarm 5. Steam temperature limit control reset button OUT. rings and instrument lights remain on. Fill coils in prescribed manner, reset button, and start as usual. 

6. Air switch contacts open. Alarm rings and instrument lights remain on. Turn control switch OFF. Be sure air admission valve is fully open. Clean strainer line in atomizing air line and drain air pressure regulator. If low atomizing air pressure persists, tighten the adjusting screw at top of air pressure regulator to increase pressure. When air pressure is restored, start steam generator in prescribed manner. 

Motor Starts but Burner Does Not: 

If the fire fails to light the low temperature contacts on the stack switch do not close. After 43 to 47 seconds time delay, the outfire relay contacts open and shut down the motor. The alarm rings and instrument lights remain on. Turn control switch OFF and check following instructions: 

1. Ignition Failure: No spark visible through peephole glass, or spark is of low intensity. Rotary circuit breaker reset may be open. Spark plug electrodes may be dirty, set too close together, or too far apart. The ignition circuit may be grounded. Loose connections, chafed or broken wire which may be breaking or grounding the circuit. 

2. Low Fuel Manifold Pressure: 

Turn handle on suction line fuel filter several times. A suction leak may be cause of low fuel pressure. Turn control switch on fill to bleed lines and bring manifold pressure up to normal. 

3. Low Fuel Nozzle Pressure: Lack of water causes servo fuel control to limit fuel to nozzle. Water supply can be low enough to cause fuel solenoid valve to close. a. Be sure water pump test valve is closed, and that cover on strainer tank is tight, that three-way wash-out valve is fully open, and that the drain valves are tightly closed. b. Open and close the water by-pass regulator several times to free it of possible sticking due to sediment in the water. c. High feed water temperature or leaky water line connection may cause the water pump to become air bound or vapor bound. 

Irregular Trainline Pressure: 

1. Burner cycle on and off.

Insufficient water delivery Check causes the steam generator to run into superheat. water pump output as previously instructed. 

Repeated cycling which would cause superheating may cut out high temperature contacts of steam temperature limit

control, shutting down generator and sounding alarm. 

2. Safety valves blow. Shut down steam generator, lower trainline pressure setting of water by-pass regulator and start steam generator again.

Regarding standby heat modification as made to some of the OK-4740 Steam Generators. 

This is a program whereby the boiler is operated in the standby position of the control switch. With the boiler on standby it will fire up and heat the water in the storage tank to a temperature governed by the setting on the aquastat. Then the fire will go out, but the boiler water pump will continue to run and circulate the water, when temperature of water goes below the setting of the aquastat, then the fire will light and heat water to the aquastat setting again. The fire will only be on at intervals, but the boiler water pump will run continuously. 

Following is a list of valves on these steam generators showing the positions for normal running operation, and for standby operation. The two valves X and Y that were added to the piping at the water storage tank. 

The following valves must be OPEN during normal operation of the steam generator:

The following valves must be CLOSED during normal operation of the steam generator: #2 Coil Blowdown Valve and Switch 

#4 Fill Test Valve #6 Layover Connection Shut-Off Valve #8 Manual Water By-Pass Valve #10 Steam Admission Valve to Radiation (Open in cold weather) 

# 12 Steam Separator Blowdown Valve #14 Washout Inlet Valve #16 Washout Inlet Valve # 18 Water Pump Test Valve # 20 Water Suction Line Drain Valve # 22 Water Treatment Tank Drain Valve # 56 Return Line Valve (Standby) #58 Stop and Check Valve Drain (Standby)

Santa Fe 

4915 VAPOR HOT WATER HEATERS 

STILET PART III-SECTION 5

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

The 4915 Vapor Water Heater, designed and manufactured by the Vapor Car Heating Company is used on some Diesel switchers and road switchers for heating the engine cooling water to prevent the water from freezing when the locomotives are tied up at the end of shift or day's work. 

The operation of this heater is completely automatic after it has been started. The motor runs continuously while the burner cycles on and off. 

The heater part of the unit consists of two water jackets connected in series. Water is pumped into the outer water jacket and then through the inner water jacket. Heat is furnished by the combustion of diesel fuel oil, which is sprayed through the fuel sprayhead into the fire pot. Here the fine oil spray mixes with air supplied by the blower and is ignited by a continuous electric spark. The hot gases flow past the inner surface of the inner water jacket and then around to wipe over the outer surface of the inner water jacket and the inner surface of the outer water jacket. 

A single electric motor drives the blower fan, fuel pump and the water circulating pump. An immersion thermostat keeps the water temperature returning to the heater within its control range of 110° to 130° F.

A small rectifier is used with the D. C. heaters to permit operation from a 110 volt A.C. line. The rectifier changes the input A. C. voltage to 64 volts D.C. for heater operation. 

If there is a failure in the A.C. yard or power line, the relay in the rectifier automatically switches the heater operation to batteries. Then when the A.C. is again available the heater operation is automatically transferrred from batteries to rectifier. 

OPERATION OF STANDBY HEATER 

When locomotive is to be tied up at the end of shift or day's work, the standby heater must be placed in operation before diesel engine is shut down. 

To Place Heater in Operation: 

1. Close main switch. This should start motor. If motor fails to start, press re-set button and check both 15 amp. fuses. 

2. Rotate fuel filter handle at least one revolution clockwise. 

3. Press start button. This puts control circuit in operation. However, fire will not light when radiator water is still warm. Press test button located below main switch to determine that fire will light when water temperature decreases. If fire fails to light,

Heater When locomotive is to be tied up for a period greater than 8 to 10 hours, use of 110-volt AC external power supply. 

To Place Heater in Operation Using 110 Volt A.C. Power Supply: 

1. Plug in 110-volt A. C. to receptacle located behind left front step of locomotive. 

2. Turn on rectifier at toggle switch located through door in left side of front hood directly in front of left cab seat. Observe that indicating light is burning. If this indicating light is not burning, report this condition immediately. 

3. Place heater in operation as outlined above. 

To Cut Out Standby Heater: 

1. Open main switch of standby heater. 

2. Turn off rectifier. 

3. Disconnect 110-volt power supply. 

4. Start diesel engine in accordance with outstanding instructions. 

RUNNING ATTENTION 

1. Fuel pressure should read 100 lbs. on the gauge, when unit is firing. 

2. If rectifier is used, check the indicating light for burning. 

3. Check damper to see that it is properly adjusted.

TROUBLE SHOOTING 

1. If, motor is running there is no fire and the temperature of the water is below 1200 so that the aquastat or immersion thermostat is calling for heat. a. If there is fuel pressure: (Should be 100 lbs. on gauge) 

1. See that the nozzle is clean and produces an even spray pattern.

PLATE 7500-3 CONTROL PANEL MAIN 

SWITCH STUP 

TEST BUTTON RESET BUTTON START BUTTON 

7503 503

2. See that there is sufficient spark at the electrodes, gap should be 1/8 inch. Lack of spark may be due to the magneto contacts not opening properly, they should be set at .018 inch. 

3. See that the electrodes are just out of the spray pattern. 

4. See that the spray pattern has approximately 1/4 inch inside clearance all around the stabilizing cone. 

5. See that the damper is properly adjusted. 

6. See that the aquastat or immersion thermostat is calling for heat. b. If there is no fuel pressure: 

1. Check fuel flow through bleeder valve under gauge (Bottom side of tee use Allen wrench.) 

2. Check operation of fuel solenoid valve. 

3. Check fuel pump coupling by removing the inspection cover on air duct. 

4. Check for fuel suction leak in line. 

2. If motor does not run. 

A. See that the motor overload reset is made. 

B. Check both fuses. 

C. Check power supply to unit. 

Note: If trouble continues check the alignment between fuel pump and water pump and between oil pump and water pump. 

3. A rapid rise in the temperature may be caused by air in the water system causing poor circulation. Bleed the air from the water system through the plug or pet cock located on top of the heater near the water outlet, or else on the water pump whichever is higher. Be sure that the water circulating pump is operating.

STEAM GENERATING TENDERS 

NOS. 9000-9004 INC. 

PART III-SECTION 6

GENERAL DATA Weight of Steam Tender (loaded). 

Fuel oil capacity. 

Water capacity .195,600 lbs. .1,050 gallons .8,500 gallons

GENERAL DESCRIPTION 

Steam generating tenders, No.

9000-9004, contain two 

Model CFK 4225 Vapor-Clarkson steam generators. Two dynamos 

(turbine-generator sets) for supplying electrical power needed to run the steam generators are located just outside the steam generator room on top of the water tank. The turbines are piped into the steam lines from both steam generators so that either turbine may be operated from steam produced by either steam generator. It requires all the steam from one steam generator to operate the turbine, then it runs at low speed. 

The fuel tank is mounted on the underframe of the unit and is filled from either side through a filler pipe. An emergency fuel cut-off valve is located beneath the tank and may be tripped by using any one of three cables located on each side of unit and one in the unit near the fuel pump. The water storage tank is filled from a manhole in the top of the tank. Outside of the front of the unit to the right of the doorway is a receptacle to accommodate a jumper cable so that batteries of the locomotive unit ahead can be trainlined to the tender unit to operate one steam generator (two steam generators, if two locomotive units are jumpered together). 

Steam Generator Tender Operation (Coupled to Diesel Locomotive): 

With all switches in the "OFF" position, and tender jumper cable plugged into locomotive rear unit, examine cable plug-in to see that it is secured with chain. On locomotives having two lead units (double end operation) duplex cables are used. Connections are made, in parallel, using jumper connection on steam generating unit, completing connection to shunts of starting contactors on units to which steam generator unit is coupled. 

1. Close main steam generator switch on locomotive rear unit. On locomotives having two lead units (double end operation) the jumper cable connection establishes this circuit. 

2. Connect switch marked "No. 1 Steam Generator" or "No. 2 

Steam Generator" to "Battery" and throw light switch to "Battery". 

3. Operate steam generator according to instructions in 

Part III, Section 2 of this book. 

As soon as steam pressure reaches a minimum of 150 lb. 

Blow Down Separator to Get Rid of Excess Water, then open

the steam valve to turbine desired to operate this steam genarator. 

When the turbine operates smoothly and is warmed up properly, connect this turbine to circuit of the steam generator being fired by throwing the switch on the back panel to that steam generator. Change switch on front panel from "Battery" to "Dynamo" while steam generator control switch is moved momentarily to "OFF" position. The steam generator motor is now being operated from the steam turbo-generator. 

The second steam generator may now be fired by using current from locomotive battery or the second turbo-generator. 

The steam generators are wired from the switchboards in such a way that they may be operated from No. 1 dynamo, No. 2 

dynamo, or the locomotive battery, BUT NO TWO SOURCES OF CURRENT CAN BE USED ON ONE STEAM GENERATOR AT THE SAME TIME. 

When opening train line valve to put steam into train, have steam generator at full capacity and open valve gradually so as to keep steam pressure for turbine above minimum of 150 lb. 

If trainline pressure cannot be maintained over 100 lb. the dynamo should be shut off and the steam generator operated from locomotive battery until pressure is again over 150 lb. It may be practical to throttle trainline valve and keep pressure for operating turbine up to 150 lb. 

When both steam generators are operated from the locomotive battery continuously the batteries of the two rear locomotive units should be "trainlined". 

Steam turbines should exhaust to atmosphere during hot weather or when water in tank becomes too hot (150 degrees). 

AUXILIARY EQUIPMENT Air Reservoir: 

Air for this reservoir is supplied from the main reservoir on the Diesel locomotive. The reservoir is located at the right front corner of the steam tender and supplies air for atomization of fuel going into the steam generators. 

Fuel Measuring Gauge: 

A fuel measuring gauge marked in gallons is located near the rear wall of the steam generator room at floor

Alarm Signals: 

The steam tender is equipped with the standard alarm signals so that in case of steam generator failure the alarm bell will ring and green signal light will burn in the steam tender and each unit of the Diesel locomotive. On locomotives having two lead units (double end operation) the alarm circuit is not established rendering the alarm light and bell inoperative, due to no control jumper cable. It is possible to determine a defective condition by visual observation of the safety devices which automatically shut steam generator down.
